- **Nigeria Police Recruitment** (Nigeria Police Force constable recruitment aptitude test, `/nigeria-police-recruitment/*`, landing `/nigeria-police-recruitment-practice-test`): `data/nigeria-police-questions.json` (200 original questions: English grammar and usage 32, vocabulary, idioms and comprehension 28, number and algebra 32, word problems and data 24, logical and verbal reasoning 20, the 1999 Constitution and government 28, fundamental rights and the law 16, the Nigeria Police Force and policing 20), test IDs 13201-13204 (50 questions each), sets 13201-13205 (by topic), state `NGPOLICE`, prefix `NGPOL-`, 70% pass (Tigertest's target: the Police Service Commission publishes no question count, time or pass mark; guides report 60 to 100 questions in up to an hour on English, maths, general knowledge and current affairs; the 2025/2026 exercise sat it on 28 to 30 April 2026, and in 2024 JAMB ran it as a CBT). 8/7/8/6/5/7/4/5 per test, no spares. GK lane in its batch: civics, the Constitution (ss. 1-9, 35, 214-216, Third Schedule), the Police Act 2020 and the ACJA 2015; no current affairs or office-holders. Built by `tools/next100/ngpolice/build.py`; sources in `tools/next100/ngpolice/sources.md`. Region NG, hub group "Nigeria recruitment tests", `[data-theme="ngpolice"]` deep police indigo (235 65% 30%), teacher-led, borrows the police tiger.
- **Nigerian Army Recruitment** (written and aptitude test practice for Regular Recruits Intake, SSC and DSSC selection, `/nigerian-army-recruitment/*`, landing `/nigerian-army-recruitment-practice-test`): `data/nigerian-army-questions.json` (200 original questions: grammar 28, vocabulary 24, arithmetic 32, algebra/shapes/statistics 24, verbal and logical reasoning 24, Nigerian history to independence 28, the Nigerian Army and armed forces 20, Africa/ECOWAS/AU 20), test IDs 13401-13404, sets 13401-13405 (by subject), state `NGARMY`, prefix `NGARMY-`, 70% pass (Tigertest's target: the Army publishes no syllabus, count or pass mark, and its 90th and 91st RRI notices said no pre-selection CBT, though the 89th announced one; recruit screening is in the state of origin, officers go before a selection board with academic tests and an interview). 7/6/8/6/6/7/5/5 per test, no spares. GK lane in the Nigeria recruitment family: Nigerian history to 1963, the armed forces, Africa/ECOWAS/AU; static facts, no office-holders. Built by `tools/next100/ngarmy/build.py`; sources in `tools/next100/ngarmy/sources.md`. Region NG, hub group "Nigeria recruitment tests", `[data-theme="ngarmy"]` grey army olive (80 18% 30%), borrows the ASVAB tiger.
- **CDCFIB Recruitment** (Civil Defence, Correctional, Fire and Immigration Services Board recruitment CBT, `/cdcfib-recruitment/*`, landing `/cdcfib-recruitment-practice-test`): `data/cdcfib-questions.json` (200 original questions: the four services and the Board 32, fire safety, first aid and emergencies 20, Nigerian geography 24, environment and disasters 20, English grammar 24, vocabulary 20, numerical ability 36, logical reasoning 24), test IDs 13301-13304 (50 questions each), sets 13301-13305 (by topic), state `NGCDCFIB`, prefix `NGCDCFIB-`, 70% pass (Tigertest's target: the Board publishes no question count, time, subject split or pass mark; guides report 100 questions for the November 2025 online, AI-proctored CBT and some candidates reported 40). 8/5/6/5/6/5/9/6 per test, no spares, weights Tigertest's own. GK lane of the batch: the NSCDC, NCoS, FFS and NIS (their Acts, duties and history), fire safety and first aid, Nigerian geography and environment; no current affairs or office-holders. English, maths and reasoning checked against the ngpolice, ngarmy, ngcustoms, trcnpqe, ngfrsc and jambeng banks. Built by `tools/next100/cdcfib/build.py`; sources in `tools/next100/cdcfib/sources.md`. Region NG, hub group "Nigeria recruitment tests", `[data-theme="cdcfib"]` muted brick red (10 30% 40%, kept 10 points lighter than apgov at hue 5), borrows the firefighter tiger.
- **Nigeria Customs Recruitment** (Nigeria Customs Service recruitment CBT, `/nigeria-customs-recruitment/*`, landing `/nigeria-customs-recruitment-practice-test`): `data/nigeria-customs-questions.json` (200 original questions: English grammar 32, vocabulary and comprehension 28 (three short passages), arithmetic and duty sums 28, algebra, ratio and data 24, reasoning 20, customs, trade and ports 28, economics and ECOWAS trade 20, world geography 20), test IDs 13501-13504 (50 questions each), sets 13501-13505 (by subject), state `NGCUSTOMS`, prefix `NGCUS-`, 70% pass (Tigertest's target: the Service publishes no count, time or pass mark; its September 2025 press release describes an online CBT on a laptop or desktop with webcam, a pre-test two days before and an extra CBT for Superintendent applicants). 8/7/7/6/5/7/5/5 per test, no spares. GK lane in the batch-5 recruitment family: customs, trade, ports, economics, ECOWAS trade, world geography; no current affairs or office-holders; maths without a calculator. Built by `tools/next100/ngcustoms/build.py`; sources in `tools/next100/ngcustoms/sources.md`. Region NG, hub group "Nigeria recruitment tests", `[data-theme="ngcustoms"]` muted slate teal (184 20% 33%), borrows the security guard tiger.
- **TRCN PQE** (Nigeria Teachers Registration Council Professional Qualifying Examination, `/trcn-pqe/*`, landing `/trcn-pqe-practice-test`): `data/trcn-pqe-questions.json` (200 original questions on the TRCN PQE curriculum guide's five core areas, 20% each: Pedagogy as foundations of learning and policy 20 and planning, methods and classroom management 20; Literacy as reading and vocabulary 20 and grammar, writing and communication 20; Numeracy as number and arithmetic 20 and geometry, data, algebra and teaching maths 20; ICT as digital skills and mobile learning 20 and ICT in teaching and digital citizenship 20; Safeguarding as child protection and reporting 20 and safe schools and professional conduct 20), three full-length tests: test IDs 13601-13603 (60 questions each, like the real PQE's 60 in 60 minutes), sets 13601-13605 (one per area), state `TRCNPQE`, prefix `TRCN-`, 60% pass (Tigertest's target: TRCN publishes no pass mark; guides say 50%). 6 per category per test; the last two items of each pool (20 in all) are training-only. Nigerian policy facts are static (TRCN Act 1993, UBE Act 2004, NPE 2014, Child Rights Act 2003, Data Protection Act 2023); no fees, dates or office-holders. Built by `tools/next100/trcnpqe/build.py`; sources in `tools/next100/trcnpqe/sources.md`. Region NG, hub group "Nigeria teacher registration exams", `[data-theme="trcnpqe"]` muted slate teal (170 20% 38%), borrows the Praxis tiger, teacher-led.

**State variants** (real estate CA, NJ, TX, FL, NY, PA, IL, OH, GA, NC, MI, VA, WA, AZ, TN and LA; life & health and P&C insurance TX, FL, CA, NY, PA, IL, OH, GA, NC, MI, NJ, VA and WA) are full `ExamConfig`s with `variantOf` and `jurisdiction`, listed in `EXAM_VARIANTS` (not `EXAMS`, so the catalog, sitemap, emails and theme see one family). Each has its own ID range (CA test 3901 and sets 3901-3904, NJ 3951 and 3951-3954, TX 4001, FL 4051, NY 4101, each with sets +1 to +4), pseudo state code (`REALESTATE_CA`) and question prefix (`RECA-`), so progress and mastery never mix across states and no migration is needed. Store lookups (`getExamById`, `getExamForTestId`, `getExamByStateCode`...) search `ALL_EXAMS`; `getExamByPath` returns the family. Pages live at `app/real-estate/[state]/*` via `useExamVariant()` (unknown or uppercase codes 404). `components/exam/ExamStatePicker.tsx` uses the DMV onboarding sentence, but its dropdown lists only the family's variant states plus "Other state" (stored as `OTHER`) so it never implies coverage we lack: `ExamStatePrompt` is a card on the family dashboard only until a state is chosen, then `ExamStateLine` is one line in the hero with a Change control. The choice is the real state code in `examStates[familyId]` (synced to `users/{uid}.examStates`, never touches the DMV `selectedState`); "Other state" (and any older saved non-variant code, which reads as Other) stays on the national bank, labeled national-only. Opening a state dashboard records it, and the family dashboard forwards to a remembered state that has a variant. Banks come from the reviewed handoff forms via `tools/build_re_state_banks.py` (one full-length test per state: CA 150, NJ 110, each item the form's state-resolved question, `sourceQuestionId` kept). TX (120: 80 national, 40 Texas, Pearson VUE scored counts since Jan 2026), FL (100, 75% pass, DBPR outline) and NY (75, weighted to the DOS 77-hour course; DOS publishes no count or pass mark) come from `tools/build_state_variant.py realestate <ST> <dir>` instead: national items picked for the state (conflicting ones excluded, some reworded for state law, keys never changed) plus state-law items `RE<ST>-<ST><SEG>-NNN`; it prints the category counts that are the config's blueprint. Each state's official sources and national picks/exclusions are in `docs/re-state-sources/`. Insurance variants are built the same way (`tools/build_state_variant.py insurance|pnc <ST> <dir>`, ids `LH<ST>-`/`PC<ST>-`, state codes `LIFEHEALTH_TX`, `PNC_CA`...): L&H TX 130 (100 general + 30 Texas, Pearson VUE), FL 150 (the 2-15 exam), CA 150 (CDI's combined life and A&H exam, 60% pass); P&C TX 130, FL 150 (2-20, capped from 160), CA 150 (combined, 60%). ID blocks L&H 4150/4200/4250, P&C 4300/4350/4400; routes `app/life-health-insurance/[state]/*` and `app/property-casualty-insurance/[state]/*`; sources in `docs/insurance-state-sources/`. The second batch (real estate PA to TN, insurance NY to WA, Oct 2026) is built the same way, each `national.json` carrying its `"total"`; ID blocks of 50 from 4450 (real estate PA, IL, OH, GA, NC, MI, VA, WA, AZ, TN), 4950 (L&H NY, PA, IL, OH, GA, NC, MI, NJ, VA, WA) and 5450 (P&C, same order). States whose exam is one integrated form (MI, OH, PA, NY, VA, WA, GA insurance) place state items where the outline cites state law; states that test each line separately (NC, NJ, IL insurance) get both lines on one form, capped at 150 keeping the general to state ratio; where the parts pass at different marks (VA, IL real estate) `passPct` is the stricter one. State banks register the same way, through `registerExamBanks()` in `lib/banks/{realEstate,lifeHealth,propertyCasualty}States.ts`, which only the `[state]` pages (and `lib/banks/all.ts`) import, so other exams never bundle them. Louisiana (Nov 2026, Pearson VUE, 80 national + 55 state items, ID block 5950 after the P&C blocks) leaves out the common-law national items that do not fit its civil law (joint tenancy, life estates, deeds of trust, warranty deed types, adverse possession, equitable title) and adds Civil Code items (component parts, usufruct, servitudes, community property, acquisitive prescription, authentic act, lesion, redhibition, earnest money, reconduction, forced heirs). To add a state: build its bank, add a variant config and an entry in its family module, then re-run `scripts/build-training-parts.ts`; to add a family, add `[state]` wrappers under its slug.

**Translated exams.** An exam is offered in another language by a translated bank registered from the exam's `lib/banks/<examId>.ts` (`TRANSLATED_BANKS` in `lib/examData.ts`, same questionIds, categories and answer keys, so tests, sets and progress are identical across languages) plus `ExamConfig.translations[lang]` (name, labels, set names, category labels, dashboard copy). The shared exam pages read everything through `useLocalizedExam()` (`hooks/useLocalizedExam.ts`): `examLanguage()` picks the person's global `language` when the exam has it and English otherwise, `localizeExam()` merges the copy, and `lib/examUi.ts` holds the page strings that are not in `i18n/*` (add a language there too). The footer switcher shows on the exam's app pages, limited to `examLanguages(exam)`. A test keeps the language it was started in (questions are stored on the session); training and stats follow the switch at once. Fix an answer key in both banks. Translated so far: CDL (es, hand-written landing `app/es/examen-practica-cdl`), Food Handler (es, vi, ko), CDL Endorsements, OSHA 10, Forklift, CNA and EPA 608 (es, landing `/es/examen-epa-608`; temperatures written `°F`), Spain DGT (es-ES, landing `/es/test-dgt-permiso-b`), Portugal IMT (pt-PT, landing `/pt/testes-codigo-imt`), Greece (el-GR, landing `/el/test-kok-diploma-odigisis`), the Sweden theory test (sv-SE, landing `/sv/teoriprov-korkort-b`), the Denmark theory test (da-DK, landing `/da/teoriproeve-bil`), the Finland theory test (fi-FI, landing `/fi/ajokortin-teoriakoe`), the Hungary KRESZ test (hu-HU, landing `/hu/kresz-teszt`), the Romania DRPCIV test (ro-RO, landing `/ro/chestionare-drpciv-categoria-b`), the Belgium theory test in Dutch and French (nl-BE, landing `/nl/theorie-examen-rijbewijs-b-oefenen`; fr-BE, landing `/fr/examen-theorique-permis-b`) and, the other way round, the German theory test in English (en-DE, landing `/en/fuehrerschein-theorie-test`). All non-CDL translations are machine translations awaiting native review. Every exam but CDL keeps its translated copy in `lib/examTranslations/<exam>.<lang>.json` (loaded through `examTranslation()`, which checks the five hero subtitles) and its landing content in `lib/landings/<exam>.<lang>.json`, rendered by `components/exam/TranslatedExamLanding.tsx` from a three-line page at the translation's `landingPath` (`/es/examen-cna`, `/vi/thi-thu-the-food-handler`, `/ko/food-handler-siheom`...). English landings list every language through `examLandingAlternates()` (`lib/examSeo.ts`). `lib/examUi.ts` and `ExamLandingHero` carry en, es, vi and ko; the exam category labels in `i18n/{es,vi,ko}.ts` are translated for the translated exams (results pages read them). Landing screenshots are `public/landing/<examId>-{mobile,desktop}-<lang>.png`. Validate a new translated bank against its English one (same ids and keys, every text field translated, "both/all/none" options still caught by the shuffle guard) before wiring it.

**Native-language exams** (Nov 2026, phase 0 of `tools/next100/LANGUAGES.md`). An exam whose own language is not English is a normal registry exam with `ExamConfig.language` (base bank and copy in that language), `locale` ("de-DE"), `english` (the names every English surface uses: catalog, `/tests`, All tests menu, llms.txt, admin) and `review` per language (`machine`, `model`, `native`); `translations` may now hold any language, so a German exam's English version is `translations.en` on the same id. `examLanguages()` puts the base first, `examLanguage()` falls back to the base (an English-UI person opening the German exam gets German questions), and `localizeExam()` leaves the base unchanged. The UI runs in seventeen languages (en, es, vi, ko, zh, de, fr, it, pl, nl, pt, sv, da, fi, hu, ro, el): `i18n/{de,fr,it,pl,nl,pt,sv,da,fi,hu,ro,el}.ts` are machine-translated pending native review (the six Nordic, Hungarian, Romanian and Greek ones added Nov 2026, with no region switched to them yet), carry no category names (`createT` falls back to English for any missing key) and leave DMV-only blocks English. `contexts/LanguageContext.tsx` keeps the stored choice as `preferredLanguage` and exposes the page's `language` from `lib/pageLanguage.ts`: a native exam's pages run in its language, DMV pages (`isDmvPath`) only in `DMV_LANGUAGES` (en, es, vi, ko, zh; anything else reads English, since the DMV has no German questions), a country homepage in its region's `lang`; it sets `html lang`. The footer switcher offers `DMV_LANGUAGES` on DMV pages. Results and the DMV stats page read the exam's own `categoryLabels` before `i18n` categories. Per-language tables: `TEST_SET_NAMES` and `trainingPartName` (`lib/exams.ts`), `EXAM_UI` (`lib/examUi.ts`, zh included), `COPY` (`ExamLandingHero`), `SAMPLE_COPY` and `RELATED_COPY` (`ExamSampleQuestions` and `ExamRelatedTests` take `lang`), `CARD_FOOTERS` (`lib/shareCards.ts`, exam cards in the exam's language, never ko or zh), the shuffle guard's per-language list (`lib/shuffleOptions.ts`). `TranslatedExamLanding` renders a native exam's base landing too (`lang` = base, URL = `landingPath`), takes `samples` and `related`, uses `examLandingLocale()` for og:locale and inLanguage, `facts.source` from the landing JSON, and EUR (or the country's currency) on EU exams; an unwired exam's landing is a 404, not a failed build. `examLandingAlternates()` keys hreflang by locale ("es-ES"; US translations keep "es") with x-default on the translation written for the exam's own country. Regions carry `lang` and `locale` (`REGION_HREFLANG` derives from them): every region is English except Germany (`de`, `app/de/layout.tsx`), Spain (`es`), Portugal (`pt`), Sweden (`sv`, `app/se/layout.tsx`; the `/sv` landings get `app/sv/layout.tsx`), Denmark (`da`, `app/dk/layout.tsx`; the `/da` landings get `app/da/layout.tsx`), Greece (`el`, `app/gr/layout.tsx`; the `/el` landings get `app/el/layout.tsx`), Finland (`fi`, `app/fi/layout.tsx`, which also wraps the `/fi` landings), Hungary (`hu`, `app/hu/layout.tsx`, which also wraps the `/hu` landings), Romania (`ro`, `app/ro/layout.tsx`, which also wraps the `/ro` landings) and Belgium (`nl`, though its test is in English). `components/home/homeCopy.ts` holds the homepage, catalog card and country pill strings per language (en and de), `lib/landingContent.ts` the landing JSONs a non-English homepage quotes under "About each test", and a homepage hides its country CTA and local section until that test is in the catalog. `CountryPills` shows the current country, the visitor's own, the last picked and the five largest (`LARGEST`), with "More countries" opening a plain-link list of the rest. Tooling: `validate_bank.py --lang xx` (language's banned phrases and connectors, `--len-scale`, default per language), `validate_translation.py <base> <xx> --lang xx`, and the "Native-language exam" section of `.claude/skills/exam-bank/references/wiring.md`.

To add an exam: add an `ExamConfig` to `lib/exams.ts`, a bank module `lib/banks/<examId>.ts` (named by the exam id, and a line in `lib/banks/all.ts`) imported at the top of `app/<slug>/{stats,drill}/page.tsx` (the shared test and practice routes load it themselves), a `[data-theme]` block in `app/globals.css`, the category keys to `types/index.ts` and `i18n/*.ts`, and the `app/<slug>/` wrappers (layout, redirect, dashboard, drill, stats; no test or practice folders) and landing. Every blueprint count times `testCount` must fit inside that category's pool. Training sets are topic sets with topic names (see "Training sets are named by topic"); every category belongs to exactly one set and each set's `size` is its pool size. Then run `npx tsx scripts/build-training-parts.ts`. The `exam-bank` skill (`.claude/skills/exam-bank/references/wiring.md`) is the full checklist; keep it in step when the wiring changes, since every new exam is built from it. Wrappers stay thin: a change one exam needs goes in the shared `components/exam/*` piece or an `ExamConfig` field, so every exam, new or old, keeps the same dashboard, results and landing.

### v2 exams (SAT and the other sectioned, timed exams)

`lib/v2/`, `components/v2/`, `store/useV2Store.ts`, `data/v2/` and `app/sat/` are a second engine that shares nothing with the v1 flow above (no `useStore`, no `lib/exams.ts`, no `QuestionCard`). It exists for exams the v1 shape cannot express: sections and modules with a clock, free navigation with flagging, passages beside the question, KaTeX, figures, multi-select and numeric (student-produced) answers, adaptive module 2, scaled scores.
- **Question model** is `QuestionV2` in `lib/v2/types.ts`, documented with examples in `data/v2/SCHEMA.md`. Formats: `single`, `multi` (select all that apply, or `select: N` for "Choose two"), `numeric`, `order` (tap steps into sequence), `highlight` (`{{phrase}}` markers in the passage or stem become tappable), `matrix` (`rows` x `columns`, one choice per row), `cloze` (`[[n]]` dropdowns in the stem from `blanks`) and `bowtie` (three `groups`: 2 actions, 1 condition, 2 parameters). Every answer is still a `number[]` or a numeric string (matrix and cloze hold -1 in unanswered slots), so sync, merge and analytics are format-blind. `lib/v2/grading.ts` has `isAnswered` (anything entered), `isComplete` (every row, blank and step filled, `select` met: drills enable Check on it and linear sections require it before Next) and `isCorrect` (all or nothing; order, matrix and cloze compare in sequence, the rest as sets). The inputs live in `components/v2/FormatInputs.tsx` (tap only, no drag, phone first); highlight and cloze render through `RichText`'s `renderSlot`. A case-study item carries both `stimulusId` and its own `passage`, and `getStimulus` shows the update ("New information") above the case. Eliminator and A-F keyboard picks apply to single and multi only. Options go up to 6 (A-F).
- **Source-built banks.** NCLEX, EMT, Security+, A+ and AWS are generated by `tools/build_v2_bank.py <exam>` from per-domain source files in `tools/<exam>/` (plus `stimuli.json` for case studies): it assigns ids so each test interleaves its domains (`<PREFIX>-T1-001..`, `-T2-..`, `-D-..`; a case study stays one block, ordered by `caseStep`). Edit the sources, never the built JSON. `tools/v1_to_v2_sources.py` was the one-off split of the old v1 banks. Validate any v2 bank with `tools/validate_v2_bank.py <exam> '<plan>'`, which checks each format's shape. Banks are JSON arrays tagged by `section`, `test`, `module` and optional `variant` (`lower`/`upper` for adaptive module 2); the test builder in `lib/v2/session.ts` filters on those tags, so adding a test is content only. Rich text (`components/v2/RichText.tsx`) handles `$...$` KaTeX, bold, italic, `__underline__`, bullets and pipe tables. Figures are SVG files under `public/figures/<exam>/`.
- **Exam config** is `ExamV2Config` (`lib/v2/exams/sat.ts`, registered in `lib/v2/registry.ts`): sections, modules with time limits, calculator and reference sheet, a raw-to-scaled curve per section (`curve()` anchors, an estimate, not an official table), a composite, drills by domain, goal choices.
- **Two modes, two devices.** `ExamRunner` is the desktop simulation (full-screen, timed, review screen, break, keyboard shortcuts A-E and arrows, calculator and reference panels) and also works on phones with a fixed bottom bar. `DrillRunner` is the phone-first mastery drill (one tap checks, misses come back until mastered, same mechanics as v1 training sets). Both use `QuestionView` and go full-screen via `.v2-focus` so site chrome never competes with the question. An exam's `drillSize` (20 on SAT and ACT, unset elsewhere) trims each drill to that many exam-level questions, an even spread across its difficulty-sorted pool in `drillQuestions` (`lib/v2/training.ts`), so the dashboard count, overview copy and mastery all follow that one number (`size` on a `DrillDef` overrides it). The pools themselves stay larger (test 1 plus the drill-only `test: 0` items; held-out tests never appear).
- **Store** `useV2Store` persists to localStorage under `tigertest-v2` (sessions, results, drills, goals, `syncedUid`). `components/v2/V2Sync.tsx` (mounted by `V2Shell` on every v2 page) mirrors it to the `v2` field of `users/{uid}` via `lib/v2/sync.ts`, written with `updateDoc` so the field is replaced whole and v1 fields are untouched. On sign-in, guest data is merged into the account with the pure `mergeV2` in `lib/v2/merge.ts` (results and mastered questions are unions, the further-along in-progress test wins, account goals win); on sign-out or a different account the local copy is cleared.
- **Dashboard** (`DashboardV2`) follows the v1 exam dashboards: goal picker first, then a hero with the tiger face ladder and an N/9 step counter (one step per drill plus one per full test; a test step completes when its best score reaches the goal), on the shared `ProgressCard`. Drills draw every bank item in their domains except items from a test marked `heldOut` in the config (`heldOutTests()` in `lib/v2/training.ts`); items tagged `test: 0` are drill-only. SAT and ACT hold out Practice Test 2 so it measures readiness, not memory. A "Your stats" card at the bottom links to `/<slug>/stats` (`StatsV2`, one wrapper per exam copied from its dashboard page). It is not a question table like v1 (these exams never repeat a question); it answers "what would I score today, and where are my next points?" through `estimateScore()` in `lib/v2/scoreEstimate.ts`: (1) **Score today**, an estimate on the exam's own scale against the goal, from first-try accuracy per domain (each answered question counts once, right only if never missed; thin domains smoothed toward their section) weighted by the domain's share of a real test form (test 1, harder path), scaled by the section's curve (lower path below the adaptive threshold) and combined with the exam's own `composite.combine`; a section needs `MIN_SECTION_ANSWERS` (10) distinct answers first. (2) **Your fastest points**, every drill ranked by the composite points it is worth at `TARGET_ACCURACY` (90%), with the skills missed in it (`QuestionV2.skill`) and a Practice button. (3) Practice test scores. (4) **Mistake notebook**, every missed question as a re-answerable `V2StatsRow` (the `QuizRow` look over `QuestionView`, never changing progress). Per-question counts come from `v2QuestionPerformance()` in `lib/v2/questionStats.ts`: answered items of finished tests plus `questionStats[examId][questionId]` (`{n, w}` drill tallies in `useV2Store`, synced in `users/{uid}.v2`, merged by max, also incremented by the MCP drill tool; recorded since Oct 31 2026, older drill progress counts once from mastered and wrong-queue ids). Every number is labelled an estimate ("Estimated <exam> score", "about +70 pts", and the exam's own "Not affiliated with ..." sentence from `copy.sourceLine`) so it never reads as an official or promised score. **Free since Oct 31, 2026**: `V2_STATS_PAYWALL` in `StatsV2.tsx` is false, so every v2 stat is open to everyone and the SAT and ACT landings say "no paywall". Flipping it back restores the gate: Premium on every exam in every country (class students see everything), the estimate, the #1 topic and the tests free; topics 2+ (lock card shows how many points they are worth, capped at the room left on the scale) and the notebook past 2 rows are locked, checkout location `<examId>_stats` returning to the stats page, paywall item ids `v2_<exam>_stats_plan` / `_stats_mistakes`. Guests see their own local progress. The shell, tabs and lock are the shared `components/stats/StatsParts.tsx` pieces also used by the v1 `ExamStatsPage`. The site header on v2 paths (`getExamV2ByPath` in `lib/v2/registry.ts`) carries the exam's theme and the Your Stats icon (accounts always, guests once they have progress on that exam on the device; highlighted on the stats page).
- **SAT** (`/sat`, landing `/sat-practice-test`, catalog id `sat` in `lib/testCatalog.ts`, after GED in the "GED, college, military & nursing school entrance" group, `[data-theme="sat"]` blue): two full practice tests, fully adaptive. Files in `data/v2/sat/`: Test 1 is `rw-m1`, `rw-m2` (variant upper), `rw-t1-m2-lower`, `ma-m1`, `ma-m2` (upper), `ma-t1-m2-lower`; Test 2 is `rw-t2-m1`, `rw-t2-m2-{lower,upper}`, `ma-t2-m1`, `ma-t2-m2-{lower,upper}`. Each R&W module is 27 items in 32 min, each Math module 22 in 35 min with 6 numeric items; module 2 variant is picked from module 1 accuracy (threshold 0.6), and a section routed to the easier module 2 is scored on its `lowerScale`, capped at 610 like the real SAT's lower path (`scaleFor()` in `lib/v2/scales.ts`; the results page says so). Drill-only items (`test: 0`) are `rw-drill-{a,b}` (SAT-RW-001..080) and `ma-drill-{a,b,c}` (SAT-MA-001..100; `c` tops up data analysis and geometry). 474 original items in all; Test 2 is held out of the drills. Validate with `tools/validate_v2_bank.py`.
- **ACT** (`/act`, landing `/act-practice-test`, `[data-theme="act"]` red): enhanced 2025 format, four choices everywhere. English 50 in 35 min (5 shared passages, `__underlined__ [N]` markers), Math 45 in 50 min with calculator and a break after, Reading 36 in 40 min (4 passages, one paired), Science 40 in 40 min (6 passages with pipe tables). Two full tests plus a drill-only bank, all in object form `{ stimuli, questions }` merged by `withStimuli()` in `lib/v2/banks/act.ts`: Test 1 is `{english,math,reading,science}-{a,b}.json` (ids 101.., stimuli P1-P6 per section), Test 2 is `*-t2{a,b}.json` (ids 201.., stimuli P6+/P5+/P7+) and is held out of the drills, and `drill-{english,math-a,math-b,reading,science}.json` are `test: 0` items (ids 001.., stimuli `D1`..) with shorter passages for the phone. Questions reference `stimulusId` and ids sort in passage order. Drills are the eleven ACT reporting categories (Reading's small Integration category shares a drill with Craft and Structure); each has `legacyKey` pointing at the section drill it replaced, so `drillStateFor()` in `lib/v2/training.ts` carries old progress forward until the new drill has its own. Each section is scaled 1 to 36 with `curve()`; the composite averages English, Math and Reading, and Science reports on its own. `ExamRunner` keys `QuestionView` by `stimulusId` so a shared passage keeps its scroll across its questions. `breakAfter` on a section limits the exam's break to that section change. The OG card at `/og/act` and the exams sitemap both know about it. ACT has its own eight-expression tiger set in `public/tigers/act/`, selected through `lib/tigerAssets.ts`.
- **GED** (`/ged`, landing `/ged-practice-test`, first in the "GED, college, military & nursing school entrance" group, `[data-theme="ged"]` violet, fully free, no `premium` block): four sections, one module each, timed shorter than the real subjects and taken in one sitting with the exam's break between them: RLA 25 in 55 min (`gedReading` 15, `gedLanguage` 10), Math 25 in 60 min with calculator (`gedQuant` 11, `gedAlgebra` 14), Science 20 in 45 min with calculator (`gedScience`), Social Studies 20 in 40 min (`gedSocial`); 90 items a test, two tests with Test 2 held out, `testLength` "3 hr 20 min". The real GED is RLA 150 min with a 45-minute essay (not modeled), Math 115 (45% quantitative, 55% algebraic), Science 90 (life 40, physical 40, earth and space 20), Social Studies 70 (civics 50, US history 20, economics 15, geography 15), the assessment targets in the GED Testing Service Assessment Guide for Educators (October 2020 edition), each scored 100 to 200 with 145 passing; here each section is `percentScale` and the composite `overallPercent`, goals 55 to 80 (default 65), with a `scoreNote` that 145 is not a fixed percent. Bank `data/v2/ged/questions.json` (240 items, object form `{ stimuli, questions }` through `withStimuli()`: pools 40/30/32/38/50/50, 10 drill-only per domain, built from `tools/ged/*.json` and `tools/ged/stimuli.json` by `tools/build_v2_bank.py ged`; stimulus ids `GED-R-xx`, `GED-SC-xx`, `GED-SS-xx`). Formats: reading `single`, a few `multi` and `highlight`; language `cloze` and `single` about half and half; math `single`, `numeric` (about a third) and a few `multi`, every math item `calculator: true`; science and social `single`, a few `multi`, `numeric`, and one or two `matrix` or `order`. `drillSize` 20 and six drills, one per domain: `reading` (Basics mainIdea, findingDetail, inference, wordsInContext, authorPurpose, claimSupport, factOpinion), `language` (commaSplices, subjectVerb, apostrophes, transitions, commonlyConfused, verbTense, pronounCase, listIntroCommas), `quant` (fractionOps, decimals, percents, ratios, unitConversion, meanMedian, orderOfOperations), `algebra` (solveX, slope, distributing, substitution, inequalities, factoring, systems), `science` and `social` (no Basics); reading, language, quant and algebra items that test one of those skills carry a `basics` tag. Dashboard is 12 steps: 4 Basics, 6 drills, 2 tests. Listed in `TEACHER_LED` and `ExamClassFaq` for adult-education classes. Uses the DMV tiger until a `public/tigers/ged/` set exists.
- **Basics** (`lib/v2/basics.ts`, library split across `data/v2/basics/{skills,reading,math,science,writing,timestables,ks2,gcsescience,gcsecs,elevenplus,fsmaths,satfamily,entrance,selective,gcse}.json`, 194 skills (Nov 2026: 31 written to replace skills that opened two drills of one exam); a drill that opens with Basics lists at least four, topped up with existing skills that fit (a skill opens at most one drill of an exam, since it is learned once: never borrow a skill another drill already opens, write a new one; `tests/no-repeats.test.cjs` checks this, every drill domain and duplicate questions for every v2 exam against `tests/no-repeats-baseline.json`, which only shrinks), pinned by `tests/basics-min-skills.test.cjs`) is the free first level inside a phone drill, for students the exam-level items are too hard for: one rule a drill depends on (semicolons, slope, reading a table) as a rule card (rule, "the trick", good/bad examples, an ELI5 explanation in kid words behind the "ELI5" button) plus 2 one-line questions (`blank` with `__`, or a `stem`, with an optional `table` or a 2 to 3 sentence `passage` for reading and science skills; punctuation options show their names; options are shuffled per question by `basicsOptionOrder`). Misses come back until right, two misses bring the rule card back once. Skills are one shared library; a drill lists the ids it opens with in `DrillDef.basics` (SAT and ACT Conventions share Semicolons). TEAS uses it too: its Math and English drills are split by ATI subdomain (Numbers and Algebra, Measurement and Data, Grammar and Punctuation, Language and Vocabulary, each with `legacyKey` to the old section drill) and open with the shared cards plus seven TEAS math walkthroughs (fractionConvert, fractionOps, orderOfOperations, percentChange, scientificNotation, dosage, volume); Science has no Basics yet. 78 of the 200 TEAS items carry a `basics` tag. HESI A2, ACCUPLACER and ASVAB use it too (their `train` pages are `TrainPage`): HESI Math is split into Numbers and Operations (`hesiNumbers`) and Conversions and Dosage (`hesiConversions`), ACCUPLACER Writing into Standard English Conventions (`accConventions`) and Expression of Ideas (`accExpression`), each with `legacyKey` to the old drill and the old domain kept in `domainLabels`; HESI reading, vocabulary and grammar, every ACCUPLACER drill, and ASVAB AR, WK, PC and MK open with Basics. Skills added for them: romanNumerals, militaryTime, decimals, rounding, negativeNumbers, gcfLcm, distributing, substitution, squareRoots, rateProblems, wordProblems, factOpinion, wordParts, synonyms (walkthroughs) and commonlyConfused, pronounCase, verbTense, listIntroCommas (voiced explainers, recorded through the ElevenLabs connector and built with `--align`). 394 of their 600 items carry a `basics` tag; the science and technical drills have no Basics. `/<slug>/train?drill=k` is `TrainPage`: the `DrillOverview` (skill list, then the exam level, which opens when every skill is learned or on "skip ahead") when the drill has Basics, `&skill=id` is `BasicsRunner`, `&level=exam` is the unchanged `DrillRunner`. Learned skills are `basics[`${examId}:${skillId}`]` in `useV2Store` (synced and merged like drills). On the dashboard each drill with Basics is two numbered steps, "<drill> [Basics]" (green when every skill is learned) then the drill itself, and both count in the N/N; the exam step always links straight to the questions, so anyone who already knows the basics just taps it (the overview has no skip link). A Premium drill gates both steps, Basics included (`isDrillLocked` + `hasDrillProgress` in `lib/v2/premium.ts`); the overview, `BasicsRunner` and `DrillRunner` all show the shared `DrillLockScreen`, and a drill someone already has progress in (exam-level or a learned skill) stays open.
- **Brush up from the exam level** (`lib/v2/skillMatch.ts`, `components/v2/BasicsRuleSheet.tsx`, `components/v2/BasicsRuleCard.tsx`): a missed `DrillRunner` question offers "Brush up on the basics · <skill>", which pops the same rule sheet a Basics question's Rule button opens (shared `BasicsRuleSheet`: "Listen instead" when voiced, the `RuleCard`, ELI5, "Back to the question"). The skill comes from the question's hand-set `basics` tag (`QuestionV2.basics`, a Basics skill id; 329 of the 376 drill-pool items carry one, the other 47 have no skill for their rule, e.g. dashes, parallel structure, verb tense, and get no card). `matchBasicsSkill(question, drill)` returns the tagged skill if the drill opens with it, else an exact `skill`-label match, else null; nothing fuzzier, because cue-word matching against explanations sent comma questions to Semicolons. Tag new drill-pool items when adding them; the untagged list is also the list of Basics skills worth writing next.
- **Two ways to learn a skill, by what the skill is.** A skill that is one sentence to recognise (the 12 grammar skills and the 4 single-sentence writing skills: redundancy, concision, preciseWords, matchingTone) gets the voiced **explainer** video below. Everything else, 53 skills, is a procedure or passage work (all math, all reading, all science, the paragraph-level writing skills, Transitions) and gets a click-through **walkthrough** instead: watching a procedure does not teach it, supplying each step does. `BasicsRunner` leads the learn screen with the walkthrough when the skill has one, else the explainer when voiced, else the text card; the text card is always behind "Read it", and the rule sheet (`BasicsRuleSheet`, from the Rule button and from a missed exam-level question) offers "Walk through it, step by step" and "Listen instead" as the skill has them.
- **Basics walkthrough** (`BasicsWalkthrough` in `lib/v2/basics.ts`, `components/v2/BasicsWalkthrough.tsx`, content in each skill's `walkthrough` field): a board pinned on top (`board` text, `kind: "math"` for a large centred expression, or `passage: true` / `table: true` for the skill's own) and 3 to 7 steps under it, each either `say` (read it, Next) or `ask` (two or three options; a wrong tap shows `hint` and stays tappable, the right one shows `then`). A step's `line` is added to a work column under the board when it is done, the last such line green, so the solution builds up from the student's own taps. No voice, no clock, no score; the review after two misses and the miss feedback button ("Walk through it again") reuse it. Authoring rule for math: say the mapping (which number is c), show the search (list the pairs), check the answer; for reading and science: the steps are the strategy itself (name the point, find the signal word, put a finger on the words). Option order is `basicsOptionOrder(skillId, 100 + step, n)`. A step can also be a **drag board** (`play: "<key>"`, its `say` the line above it; done once the board is solved): `components/v2/play/BasicsPlay.tsx` maps the key to a board built on Dragology (`dragology`, MIT, declarative dragging: each board state is a step of the solution and a part can only be dragged to the next legal state), loaded with `next/dynamic` so only those steps ship the library. Every move also works as a tap and from the keyboard, and the library needs the same SVG children in every state (toggle opacity, never add or drop elements). Pilot (Nov 2026): `solveX` ends with `SolveXBoard` (3 problems, `ax + b = c`); `play_start` and `play_done` count per skill in `analytics/v2Basics` (no admin card yet).
- **Basics explainer** (`lib/v2/explainer.ts`, `components/v2/BasicsExplainer.tsx`) is a 30-second animated walk through one skill: rule, trick, a sentence that breaks it, one that follows it, "now you try" (the ELI5 picture stays on the rule card behind its button). A voiced skill is video first: opening it shows the explainer's Play screen with "Read it" to the text card, the review after two misses does the same, a miss offers "Watch the explainer", and the rule sheet behind a question's Rule button opens on a "Listen instead" link (the overlay's "Read it" drops back into that sheet); the text card keeps a "Watch it instead" link. The Play tap is the gesture phones require before sound, so `play()` is called inside it and nothing guesses about autoplay. The storyboard is built from the skill's own fields, so there is one template per example style, not one video per skill (`explainerTemplate`, no table or passage either way): Sentence (a good/bad pair) strikes the words that differ in the bad sentence and marks them in the good one; Worked (math `eg` examples with a note) shows each expression large, then the note's sentences as numbered steps as they are spoken, the last ("So ...") green as the answer. Worked exists and Factoring is voiced with it, but math skills lead with their walkthrough; do not voice more math. The voice script goes through `speakable()` in the build script (x² is "x squared", · is "times", ")(" is ", times", a leading − is "negative"), and the aligner expands each written word the same way, so "x²" takes the span of both spoken words; the screen keeps the notation. The buttons appear only for skills listed in `lib/v2/voiced-skills.json` (`hasExplainer`), which the build script rewrites from what is in `public/basics-audio/`, so a skill without a recorded voice never offers a silent explainer. One clock drives it: the mp3 in `public/basics-audio/<skillId>.mp3` with word timings in `<skillId>.json` (audio mode starts as soon as `play()` is accepted and shows "Loading sound..." while the mp3 buffers), or estimated timings labeled "no sound" when the files are missing or the audio fails. `scripts/build-basics-audio.ts` makes both files from ElevenLabs (`ELEVENLABS_API_KEY`, the `with-timestamps` endpoint) or aligns an mp3 made elsewhere to a transcript (`--align`); it reads the same `buildExplainer()` as the player, and the player ignores a manifest whose word counts no longer match, so re-run it after editing a skill's copy. Voice: "Benedict - Premium British (RP) Male" (`6q6UVsvekS0UjrUXFz79`).
- **Times Tables** (`/times-tables`, landing `/times-tables-practice-test`, `[data-theme="timestables"]` magenta at hue 300, icon `calculator`, hub group "UK school tests", fully free): times tables for primary school in England, made for a 7-year-old in Year 2/3 and built to the national curriculum order (2, 5, 10 in Year 2; 3, 4, 8 in Year 3; everything to 12 × 12 in Year 4) and the Year 4 Multiplication Tables Check (25 questions, 6 s each, 2 to 12 tables, more 6, 7, 8, 9 and 12, never a fact and its reverse in one check, no pass mark). One linear section `tt`, 25 items on a 150 s clock (untimed is a tick box); four checks: Check 1 the Year 2 tables (×1 to ×12), Check 2 Year 3 with Year 2 revision, Checks 3 and 4 the full Year 4 mix sharing no fact, Check 4 held out. Six drills of 20 (`drillSize`), every one with Basics: `twoFiveTen`, `threeFourEight`, `sixSevenNine`, `elevenTwelve`, `divide`, `stories`. Bank `data/v2/timestables/questions.json` (200 items, prefix `TT-`, numeric with `numeric.wholeNumber`, which gives a digits keypad and drops the fractions hint, plus a few single-choice) is generated: edit `tools/timestables/generate.py` (every answer computed, UK wording: pence, biscuits, Mum, "multiply by 10" never "add a zero"), then `tools/build_v2_bank.py timestables`. The 16 Basics skills (`tt*` ids, all walkthroughs, no voice) are `data/v2/basics/timestables.json`, written by `tools/timestables/basics.py`. No tiger set yet (DMV tiger).
- **KS2 SATs** (`/ks2-sats`, landing `/ks2-sats-practice-test`, `[data-theme="ks2"]` violet, hub group "UK school tests"): the Year 6 national curriculum tests in England. Three sections: reading (24 questions on 3 shared texts, story, information text and poem, 40 min), GPS Paper 1 (30, 27 min; the spelling Paper 2 is not modelled) and maths as one section of three modules (arithmetic 20 in 17 min, reasoning Papers 2 and 3, 12 in 20 min each, numeric answers, no calculator), so both tests are about 60% of the real length and share one scale. Each section is scaled 80 to 120 by `curve()` anchored at the 2026 STA conversion thresholds as fractions of our item counts (an estimate, said in `scoreNote`); the composite is the lowest of the three, so a goal of 100 means the expected standard in every subject. Test 2 held out. Five drills (reading, grammar and vocabulary, punctuation, arithmetic, reasoning), each opening with Basics (19 new `ks2*` skills in `data/v2/basics/ks2.json` plus the shared `apostrophes`), 12 dashboard steps. Bank built by `tools/ks2/build.py` (Python sources, arithmetic answers computed and asserted); Basics by `tools/ks2/basics.py`. Poems are one paragraph per line (`poem()` in `tools/ks2/common.py`) because RichText joins single newlines.
- **GCSE Maths** (`/gcse-maths`, landing `/gcse-maths-foundation-practice-test`, `[data-theme="gcsemaths"]` olive, hub group "UK school tests"): GCSE Mathematics Foundation tier (England, all boards, DfE 2013 content). Each practice test is the three papers cut to 20 items in 35 minutes (`p1` non-calculator, `p2` and `p3` calculator), 5 number, 4 algebra, 5 ratio, 3 geometry, 3 statistics per paper (Ofqual Foundation 25/20/25/15/15); Test 2 held out. 207 items in `data/v2/gcsemaths/questions.json`, built by `tools/gcsemaths/build.py` from `tools/gcsemaths/q_*.py` (edit the sources; the build rotates key letters). Five drills, one per domain, each with shared math Basics, so the dashboard is 12 steps; number and algebra drill Paper 1 items, ratio Paper 2, geometry and statistics Paper 3, each topped up with `test: 0` items. Percent score; multi-mark questions are one final answer and goal notes put a grade 4 near 65%, a grade 5 near 75%.
- **11+** (`/eleven-plus`, landing `/11-plus-practice-test`, `[data-theme="elevenplus"]` royal indigo, hub "UK school tests", region UK): GL-style multiple-choice 11+ for grammar school entry, three sections per test, English 25 in 25 min (two passages of 6 questions with shared stimuli, then 13 spelling, punctuation and grammar: "which part has a mistake / No mistake" items with `lockedOrder`, and word-choice items), Maths 25 in 25 min (13 number, 12 problem solving, five options, no calculator) and Verbal Reasoning 40 in 30 min (20 word types, 20 code and sequence types, grouped by type in paper order), 5-minute breaks. Non-verbal reasoning is not modelled. Two practice tests, Test 2 held out, 6 drills of 20 (comprehension, SPaG, number, problem solving, VR words, VR codes); four open with 13 new Basics walkthroughs in `data/v2/basics/elevenplus.json` (British spelling), 12 dashboard steps. 238 original items (6 passages, one a poem) built by `tools/elevenplus/build.py` from `tools/elevenplus/q_*.py`, which assert every computed key and check word puzzles against `/usr/share/dict/words`. Never names GL Assessment or CEM in stems. Percent score with a default goal of 80; the real test is age-standardised with local qualifying scores.
- **GCSE Maths Higher** (`/gcse-maths-higher`, landing `/gcse-maths-higher-practice-test`, `[data-theme="gcsehigher"]` violet, hub "UK school tests", region UK): three sections that are the three papers, `p1` non-calculator and `p2`/`p3` calculator, 20 items in 45 min each (3 number, 6 algebra, 4 ratio, 4 geometry, 3 statistics: the DfE Higher weightings 15/30/20/20/15), two practice tests with Test 2 held out, 5 drills (one per content area, `gcsehigherNumber`...`gcsehigherStats`), four opening with shared Basics, 11 dashboard steps. Drill-only items (`test: 0`) are in `p1` because a drill draws from one section and has no calculator. 220 original items built by `tools/gcsehigher/build.py` from `tools/gcsehigher/q_*.py` (its own builder, not `build_v2_bank.py`). Percent score, not grades.
- **GCSE Combined Science** (`/gcse-science`, landing `/gcse-combined-science-practice-test`, `[data-theme="gcsescience"]` leaf green at hue 103, icon `microscope`, hub group "UK school tests", fully free): Foundation-tier GCSE Combined Science (England) on the DfE subject content. Six drills, one per science's paper 1 and paper 2 topics (`gcsescienceBio1`, `gcsescienceBio2`, `gcsescienceChem1`, `gcsescienceChem2`, `gcsesciencePhys1`, `gcsesciencePhys2`; 10 per test from pools of 35, 15 drill-only), `drillSize` 20. Bio1, Chem1, Phys1 and Phys2 open with Basics from `data/v2/basics/gcsescience.json` (magnification, variables, relative formula mass, balancing, substituting into and rearranging physics equations, units and prefixes, resultant force), so the dashboard is 12 steps. Every physics calculation states its equation (the 2025-2027 equation sheet), and the physics section's `reference` is the sheet. Bank `data/v2/gcsescience/questions.json` (210, `GCSES-T1-..`, `-T2-..`, `-D-..`) built by `tools/gcsescience/build.py` from `tools/gcsescience/*.py`; sources in `tools/gcsescience/sources.md`.
- **GCSE Computer Science** (`/gcse-computer-science`, landing `/gcse-computer-science-practice-test`, `[data-theme="gcsecs"]` deep teal, hub "UK school tests", region UK): two sections that are the two papers every board sets, `p1` computational thinking and programming (30 items, 45 min, a pseudocode reference panel: ← assignment, zero-based arrays, explicit ENDIF/ENDWHILE) and `p2` computer systems (30 items, 40 min), 5-minute break; per test 10 algorithms, 13 programming, 7 logic and languages, 10 data representation, 9 systems, 7 networks and security, 4 impacts (DfE subject content, updated March 2025). Code is shown as a Line/Code pipe table (rich text has no code blocks); numeric answers are denary only (the input takes 7 digits). Insertion sort (OCR) and SQL (AQA, drill-only) are included. 202 original items built by `tools/gcsecs/build.py` from `tools/gcsecs/q_*.py`; Basics by `tools/gcsecs/basics.py`. Percent score, not grades.
- **Functional Skills Maths Level 2** (`/functional-skills-maths`, landing `/functional-skills-maths-level-2-practice-test`, `[data-theme="fsmaths"]` rust, hub "UK school tests", region UK): DfE 2018 Level 2 subject content and Ofqual's 25% non-calculator rule. Two practice papers (Test 2 held out), each Section A non-calculator (10 items, 20 min) then Section B calculator (30 items, 70 min) with a formula sheet; 4 drills (`fsmNumber`, `fsmPercent`, `fsmMeasure`, `fsmData`; per paper 9/12/12/7), all drill-only items in section `b`, each drill opening with four of 16 UK Basics walkthroughs in `data/v2/basics/fsmaths.json` (ids `fsm*`). 200 items, every answer computed in `tools/fsmaths/q_*.py`; rebuild with `python3 -I tools/fsmaths/build.py`. Pass marks vary by awarding body, so the score is percent correct (default goal 70).
- **OSSLT** (`/osslt`, landing `/osslt-practice-test`, `[data-theme="osslt"]` indigo at hue 232, icon `book`, fully free, hub group "Canada school tests"): EQAO's Ontario Secondary School Literacy Test (Grade 10, OSSD literacy requirement) without its two open responses. EQAO Framework: online, Session A about 65 min and B about 75, 37 scored questions = 35 selected-response (reading 23 on four selections: information paragraph 6, news report 5, dialogue 5, real-life narrative 7; writing 12) + a news-report written answer and an opinion essay; scale 200 to 400, 300 successful. Two sections, Reading 23 in 45 min (`ossltInfo`, `ossltNews`, `ossltDialogue`, `ossltNarrative`, one selection each per test) and Writing 12 in 20 min (`ossltIdeas` 4, `ossltOrganize` 3, `ossltConventions` 5; EQAO gives no split, this one is ours), no break; two tests, Test 2 held out; percent score, goals 60 to 85, default 75 as our estimate of a successful result (`scoreNote` says so). Bank `data/v2/osslt/questions.json` (200 items, 20 original Canadian passages `OSSLT-P01..P20` in object form through `withStimuli()`, ids `OSSLT-T1-`, `-T2-`, `-D-`), built by `python3 -I tools/osslt/build.py` from `tools/osslt/reading.py` and `writing.py` (helpers rotate keys; edit the sources). Formats: single, choose two, order, cloze (drop-down), highlight (select a sentence or word); Canadian spelling, no serial-comma or -our/-or items. Four drills of 20, each with shared Basics, so the dashboard is 10 steps: `infoNews`, `storyDialogue`, `ideasOrganize`, `conventions`; 159 of the 165 drill-pool items carry a `basics` tag. Teacher-led (Grade 10 English, OSSLC). DMV tiger.
- **EQAO Grade 9 Math** (`/eqao-grade-9-math`, landing `/eqao-grade-9-math-practice-test`, `[data-theme="eqao9"]` indigo, hub group "Canada school tests", region CA): Ontario's EQAO Grade 9 Assessment of Mathematics (MTH1W). Two sections that are the real Sessions A and B, each a 13-item stage 1 and an adaptive 12-item stage 2 (`lower`/`upper`, threshold 0.6), so 50 scored items per test in the EQAO blueprint (algebra with coding 18, number 10, data 8, geometry and measurement 8, financial literacy 6), calculator and the EQAO formula sheet as `reference` on both. Two tests with Test 2 held out, 5 drills (one per strand, `eqao9Number`...`eqao9Financial`) all opening with shared Basics, 12 dashboard steps. Drill-only items are in session `a`. 200 original items, code shown as Python in a numbered table, built by `tools/eqao9/build.py` from `tools/eqao9/q_*.py`. Percent score, not EQAO levels; from 2026-2027 the real result is 20% of the course mark.
- **NAPLAN Year 9** (`/naplan`, landing `/naplan-year-9-practice-test`, `[data-theme="naplan"]` green, fully free, hub group "Australia school tests"): ACARA's online NAPLAN without Writing. Four sections in the real order: Reading (`napReading`, 10 + 10 in 20 + 20 min, 5 items per text), Conventions of Language (`napSpelling` then `napGrammar`, 6 + 6 per module, 12 + 12 in 15 + 15 min), Numeracy non-calculator (`numeracyNC`, `napNonCalc`, 6 in 8 min, no calculator) and Numeracy calculator (`napNumber` 5, `napMeasure` 4, `napStats` 3 per module, 12 + 12 in 25 + 25 min); `breakAfter` on Reading and Conventions only. Tailored design modelled as SAT-style module 2 (`upper`/`lower`, threshold 0.6) in Test 1; Test 2 is a fixed form held out of the drills. Each section is scaled 300 to 780 with `curve()` (easier path capped at 600) against ACARA's 2023 Year 9 cut points (Strong from 532 to 545, Exceeding 627 to 673); the composite averages Reading, Conventions and Numeracy (the two numeracy sections weighted by length); goals 480 to 700, default 540. Bank `data/v2/naplan/questions.json` (227 items, 11 stimuli `NAP-R-xx`, ids `NAP-T1-`, `NAP-T2-`, `NAP-D-`) is built from `tools/naplan/*.py` by `python3 -I tools/naplan/build.py` (its own builder; edit the sources, never the JSON). Spelling is proofreading and choice items (the audio dictation is not modelled), Australian spelling, metric, AUD. Six drills, `drillSize` 20: reading, spelling, grammar, noncalc, number and measure (measurement plus statistics); reading, grammar and number open with shared Basics, so the dashboard is 11 steps. A Year 7 sibling would be a separate config (`naplan7`) and folder.

Percent score; negative marking explained in `scoreNote` and on the landing, not applied. Sources in `tools/next100/neet/sources.md`.
