- These four moved from v1 in October 2026 with no users to migrate: their v1 configs, banks and pages are gone, `/<slug>/dashboard|drill|stats|training` redirect to the dashboard, and old v1 test ids (`/emt/test/1701`...) redirect in `next.config.ts`. Their category keys stay in `types/index.ts` and `i18n/*` (unused), like TEAS and HESI.
- **TEAS** alternate items: 30 of its 200 items are select all that apply (12), typed numeric (10) and ordered response (8), like the real TEAS 7's up to 15%; image hot spots are not modelled. Drill-only TEAS items are `module: 1`.
- **Premium** is the one DMV purchase (`$9.99` one-time in the US, a local price elsewhere, `users/{uid}.subscription.isPremium`), which also unlocks any v2 exam whose config has `premium`. Since Nov 10 2026 that is the 54 career, licensing, government-job and graduate-admission exams in `PREMIUM_V2_IDS` (`lib/v2/registry.ts`), which sets it for them: the last quarter of the drills and practice tests is Premium (the last test when there are two, the rest from the last drills; a one-test exam locks only drills), the v1 rule carried over. School, college-entrance and children's tests (SAT, PSAT, ACT, AP, GED, GCSE, KS2, 11+, NAPLAN, selective tests, JNVST, AISSEE, JAMB, JEE, NEET, CUET, MHT-CET, NDA, ACCUPLACER, Högskoleprovet and the rest) are never sold: a new v2 exam is free unless its id is added to that list, and John has said not to sell the kids' or GCSE tests. A student in a teacher's class for the exam is never locked (`hooks/useV2Premium.ts`), and work already started stays open. Logic is in `lib/v2/premium.ts`; `useIsPremium()` reads the flag once auth settles. `V2TestGate` fronts every v2 test route (an in-progress or already-sat test stays open), `DrillRunner` shows a lock screen for a locked drill (open anyway if the person already has their own progress in it), and `DashboardV2` locks those steps and the test step and verifies the Stripe return (`usePurchaseReturn`; checkout takes a same-site `returnPath`). v2 paywall item ids start `v2_` so the DMV paywall-abandon email skips them. Client-side gating, like the DMV. Every v1 registry exam, in every country (since Nov 2026, priced locally through `lib/pricing.ts`), sells the same purchase through their stats page: `components/exam/ExamStatsPage.tsx` shows 5 rows (2 on phones) and locks the rest like the DMV `/stats`, checkout location `<examId>_stats` (the family id for a state version), which `purchaseExam()` in `lib/purchaseAttribution.ts` credits to that exam. The purchase email (`purchaseWelcome`) leads a stats buyer back to that exam's stats (`EmailVoice.statsPath`, `/<exam>/stats` for v2 too) and otherwise the DMV unlock, and lists "full stats on every other exam on Tigertest, in every country"; nothing anywhere sells SAT or ACT access. A browser in a teacher's class for the exam (`isClassStudent()` in `lib/classProgress.ts`, quiz memberships excluded) sees every stat free, on the DMV `/stats` too.

**v1 exam locks (Nov 10 2026, "start for free"; a quarter of the dashboard since Nov 14 2026).** On every v1 registry exam the last 25% of the dashboard steps are Premium (`lib/examLocks.ts`: `lockedStepCount()` is 25% of training steps plus tests, rounded, at least one; `examSteps()` is the dashboard order and the locked steps are its tail, read through `lockedTrainingParts()` and `lockedTestIds()`), so a longer exam locks more. An 8-step exam (Motorcycle) locks steps 7 and 8, EPA 608 steps 5 and 9, Life in the UK its last set and Tests 7 and 8, CDL (13 training steps, 6 tests) its last three training steps and Tests 5 and 6; a state version (one test) locks only its last one or two training steps, so its one test stays free. v2 Premium exams follow the same count over drills plus tests (`lib/v2/registry.ts`: the last test when there are two, the rest from the last drills, so an 8-drill exam locks two drills and Practice Test 2). The DMV keeps its own rule (sets and tests 3 and 4). `hooks/useExamLocks.ts` decides per person: open for Premium, for a student in a teacher's class for the exam, and for any step the person already has work in (a started or finished test, a mastered or queued question in that part), and only once the account has loaded (`settled`), so pages that gate entry wait for it. `ExamDashboard` shows the lock on the card and opens the paywall (`useExamPaywall` in `components/exam/ExamLockScreen.tsx`, checkout location `<familyId>_dashboard`, `_test` or `_set`, item ids prefixed `exam_` so the DMV paywall-abandon email skips them, purchase verified on the dashboard by `usePurchaseReturn`); `ExamTestPage` and `ExamTrainingPage` show `ExamLockScreen` for a locked URL (its three strings are in `lib/examUi.ts` for all seventeen languages). Client-side gating like the DMV; the MCP study tools are not gated. Super Amazing Mode on a v1 exam therefore needs Premium. Catalog entries carry `pricing` ("3 free, 1 premium"), so cards read "Free + Premium" and `ExamFacts` says "free to start". Copy rule: say "Start for free" / "free to start", never that everything or every set and test is free, on v1 exams or the paid v2 exams in any language; only the free v2 exams (school and children's tests) may say everything is free. Page titles keep "Free ... Practice Test". The homepage tagline is "Any test, anywhere. Start free." `tests/exam-locks.test.cjs` pins the rule.

**Local prices** (`lib/pricing.ts`): Premium is shown and charged in the visitor's currency, both from Vercel's `x-vercel-ip-country` (`lib/server/visitorCountry.ts`): `/api/price` feeds `usePremiumPrice()` (`hooks/usePremiumPrice.ts`, cached per tab by `lib/localPrice.ts`) on every paywall and lock, and `/api/stripe/checkout` charges the same price with inline `price_data` (dollars keep `STRIPE_SINGLE_PRICE_ID`). Hand-set prices per currency, about $10 in richer countries and about $5 where that is a lot (INR, PHP, MYR, ZAR, NGN, KES, PKR and others); a country not in the table, or a currency the US Stripe account refuses (GHS), pays dollars. Off production `?country=XX` on any page tries another currency (kept in the `tt-price-country` cookie so checkout follows). The webhook stores `currency`, `country` and `amountUsdCents` (Stripe's settled dollars) on each `payments` doc, and /admin revenue reads `paymentNetUsdCents()` so every figure stays in US cents. GA4 purchase events carry the local value and currency.

**Common Mistakes** (the most-missed questions, "we know which questions everyone fails") is built nightly by `/api/cron/aggregate-stats` from every real account's training answers and finished practice tests: `globalStats/wrongQuestions` for the DMV (universal questions) and `globalStats/examWrongQuestions` for every v1 registry exam (matched by `questionIdPrefix`, a state version under its own id), plus an `available` list. A test only gets it once 20 questions each have 10 answers (`lib/communityStats.ts`); from that night on, with no code change, it shows (no header icon since #589; the header carries only Your Stats) a Common Mistakes card on the exam dashboard, a "Short on time?" block on the landing (rendered by `ExamRelatedTests`; the DMV landing's is wrapped in `CommunityGate`) and a Common Mistakes tab on the stats page (`?tab=community`, open to guests, locked past 5 rows like Your Stats). Clients read `/api/community-stats/exams` once per page load (`useCommunityAvailable()`), CDN-cached for an hour. v2 exams get it the same way: the cron reads `users/{uid}.v2` through `v2QuestionPerformance()` (held-out tests skipped so Common Mistakes never shows them), keyed by the v2 exam id, shown as a tab on `StatsV2` and a card on `DashboardV2`. The cron reads accounts in 8 parallel parts (Nov 2026: one function took 250 to 280 s for 5,300 accounts and timed out some nights): GET lists accounts once, POSTs each uid slice to the same route at `SITE_URL` with `CRON_SECRET` (`lib/server/aggregateStats.ts` scans and merges; every sum is per account, so parts add up exactly), then writes the docs. Without a secret, or if the parts fail within 30 s, it scans in one call as before.
- To add a v2 exam: an `ExamV2Config` in `lib/v2/exams/` (with `landingPath`), register it, a bank module `lib/v2/banks/<exam>.ts` that imports the JSON and calls `registerBank()` (each exam page imports it for that side effect, and the shared test, results and practice routes load it on demand, so a page bundles only its own bank; name it by the exam id), run `tools/validate_v2_bank.py` so `lib/v2/bankSizes.ts` carries its question count (the catalog reads that, never a bank), `app/<slug>/` wrappers copied from `app/sat/` (layout, redirect, dashboard, stats; never test or practice folders, which are the shared `app/[slug]` routes), a landing copied from `app/sat-practice-test/`, a catalog entry, a `[data-theme]` block, a sitemap line, and an import of its bank in `lib/server/classQuestions.ts` so the class page can show its questions.

### Question quality (Nov 2026)

Answer keys are what the product is judged on, so the banks are watched after they ship, in five layers. Shared types and the flag rules are in `lib/questionQuality.ts`.
- **CI** (`.github/workflows/question-banks.yml`, on every PR touching `data/`, `tools/` or the bank wiring): `tools/check_banks.py` (blocking: answer key matches its letter, four distinct options compared case-sensitively since capitalization and genotype questions differ only by case, no empty stem or explanation, v1 ids unique across every base bank, each translated bank `<bank>_<lang>.json` has only its base's ids with the same key and category, the zh DMV bank partial on purpose; v2 ids unique per exam, key indices in range, stimulus references resolve; and, through `tools/check_option_refs.py`, no stem or explanation that points at an option by position, "the second option", "the last choice", or "Choice B" in a v1 bank or a v2 stem, since options are shuffled on screen: only `lockedOrder` items and v2 explanation letters, which `relabelChoices()` rewrites, may do that), `tools/check_freshness.py`, `tools/build_exam_sources.py --check` and `build-training-parts.ts --check` (blocking), then the house-style `validate_bank.py` on changed v1 banks as a warning only, since older banks predate it. `check_banks.py --export <file>` writes the flat index of every question (about 56,000) that the audit reads.
- **Reports**: "Report this question" (`components/ReportExamQuestion.tsx`, its strings in its own table for all seventeen languages) under the explanation in DMV and registry training (`TrainingCard` `reportExamId`) and v2 drills (`DrillRunner`). Reasons: wrong answer, two answers, unclear, outdated, typo, other, plus a note and the option picked (bank index). `POST /api/questions/report` (known exam ids only, bots dropped, about 20 an hour per IP through `grantAnswers`) writes `questionReports/{auto}` with `status: open` and counts in `analytics/questionReports`. Quizzes keep their own report route.
- **Pick statistics**: every answered exam question, class or not, goes through `recordClassAnswerById()` to `recordQuestionPick()` (`lib/questionPicks.ts`): bank index picked (-1 for formats with no single pick), right or wrong, and whether this browser is strong on the exam (30+ answers there at 80%+, kept in localStorage `tigertest-pick-skill`). Batched to `POST /api/analytics/question-picks` every 10 s and on page hide, summed per question into `questionPicks/{examId}` (`n`, `c`, `w.{i}` wrong picks, and `s`, `sc`, `sw.{i}` for strong browsers), nothing about the sender stored, own per-IP budget. Test, internal and analytics-declined browsers and quizzes never send. Recorded from Nov 3 2026. The cron `/api/cron/question-health` (03:40 UTC) runs `flagQuestion()` over every question plus open reports and writes `globalStats/questionFlags` (worst 600 by score, counts by kind and exam): **keySuspect** (12+ strong answers and strong browsers under 50% right, or their top wrong option at 75%+ of their correct count), **splitAnswer** (40+ answers and one wrong option at 30%+ of answers and 80%+ of the correct count), **reported**. A hard or an easy question is not a problem, so "too hard" and "too easy" are no longer flags (Nov 10 2026); only the signs of a wrong or second answer are. **Flags answer themselves**: the audit's daily `--signals` run (below) judges every new flag and report the next morning and stores its decision in `questionFlagReviews/{examId}` (`FlagReview`: verdict, confidence, reason, answers at the time). `applyFlagReviews()` joins flags and reviews in the cron, the Questions tab and the daily brief: a flag reviewed "ok" is **cleared** (kept, folded away on /admin, out of every count) until the question's answers double or 180 days pass (`reviewFresh`), a confirmed problem becomes a finding with a fix PR, and a verdict too unsure to be a finding stays listed with a "Key is right" button. A question with an open report is never cleared by a review. When a finding is closed as fixed (the button, or a merged resolutions file) `resetQuestionSignals()` (`lib/server/questionFlagReviews.ts`) deletes that question's pick counts and review, since they describe the old key; closed as not a problem, `clearQuestionReview()` writes an "ok" review so it is not raised again. `tests/question-flags.test.cjs` pins the rules.
- **Freshness**: `lib/examFreshness.json` lists dated changes to real exams (outline, law, format, sunset, notice, review) with the action and source; set `done` once the bank follows it. Every exam is also due a full review 365 days after its bank last changed (`lib/landingDates.json`). `lib/examSources.json` (built by `tools/build_exam_sources.py`: the first three https URLs of every `tools/**/sources.md`, plus each freshness source, about 500) is read by `/api/cron/source-watch` (04:10 UTC, a seventh of the pages a day, `?all=1` for all): each page is cut to its sentences of 40+ characters and hashed; a page is changed when 5% or more of them differ from the baseline (files compared by bytes), kept in `sourceWatch/{sha1(url)}` until marked reviewed, which makes the latest the new baseline. Pages failing twice are listed as broken. **Nobody reads them by hand** (Nov 13 2026): `.github/workflows/source-triage.yml` (05:23 UTC daily, after the cron) exports the changed and broken pages still watched and not yet triaged (`scripts/source-triage.ts --export`), and Claude Code reads each page against the exam's bank, facts, costs and landing and decides: noise (re-baselined at once, never shown), updated (bank sources, facts and copy fixed), replaced (an unofficial or broken URL swapped in `sources.md` for an official one), or needs_person (left on /admin with its reason, retried after 7 days). Decisions go in `audits/sources/<date>-<run>.json` and `sourceWatch.triage`; a day with real changes opens a PR, which the run checks itself (typecheck, lint, tests, the bank checks; a PR opened with the workflow token starts no other workflow) and merges when all pass, then re-baselines those pages (`--apply --merged`); a failed check leaves the PR open with a comment, and a PR merged by hand re-baselines through the push job. The Questions tab shows only what `sourceNeedsPerson()` (`lib/questionQuality.ts`, pinned by `tests/source-triage.test.cjs`) keeps: untriaged or needs_person pages still listed in `lib/examSources.json`.
- **Claude audit** (`scripts/question-audit.ts`, from `.github/workflows/question-audit.yml`: everything on Mondays 06:17 UTC, and on the other days at 04:47 UTC only new statistical flags and student reports (`--signals`: no freshness, no sweep; a run with nothing new writes no report and opens no PR); needs the repository secrets `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY`): queues open reports, the top statistical flags, time-sensitive questions (years, money, limits, "currently") in exams with a dated change within 60 days or a changed source page, and a sweep of `--sweep` (1,000 in the Action) random choice questions not audited in 90 days, so the roughly 54,000 choice questions are each re-read about once a year (raise it for a faster cycle; cost scales linearly). Each is answered blind (low effort, JSON schema output) with confidence, other defensible options and any problem; a disagreement, and every reported, flagged or freshness item, goes to a second call (high effort) with the key, explanation, evidence and the exam's `sources.md`, which returns ok, wrong_key, ambiguous, outdated, unclear or typo with the exact fix. Findings of medium or high confidence go to `audits/<date>.json` and `.md`, `questionAuditFindings/{exam__id}` (a dismissed finding is not raised again for the same verdict), `questionAudits/{runId}` and `questionAuditLog/{examId}`. When there are findings, the Action runs Claude Code (`anthropics/claude-code-action`) to check each one itself, fix the bank sources (never only the built JSON), rebuild, fix the key in every translated bank, run the checks, append what it changed, write `audits/resolutions/<date>-<run id>.json` for the findings it settled (fixed, or dismissed after checking the official rule), and opens a PR (`peter-evans/create-pull-request`), which the run checks itself (typecheck, lint, tests, the bank checks) and merges when all pass and a resolutions file was written, then applies that file with `--resolve` (a merge made with the workflow token starts no push job); a failed check leaves the PR open with a comment. John chose this on Nov 13 2026: fixes to the banks need no person. **A second reviewer comes first**: before the merge, a second Claude Code run that did not write the fixes re-checks every changed question against the exam's sources and writes `verify.json`; any objection, or no verdict, leaves the PR open with a comment naming what failed (fix step, second review, checks, or no finding settled). The checks are `scripts/automerge-checks.sh`. Both fix steps (this one and the source triage) may change a question only from an official source read with WebFetch (the exam owner, the government or regulator, or the primary law, standard or manual), recorded dated in the exam's `sources.md`; prep, coaching and news sites are never enough on their own. Local: `python3 -I tools/check_banks.py --export /tmp/q.json && npx tsx scripts/question-audit.ts --index /tmp/q.json --dry-run`. The index carries what the candidate sees beside the stem (a v2 item's shared stimulus title and text, a figure's alt text) and the auditor is told a v1 sign-image question's sign from `lib/signImages.ts`; before Nov 9 2026 it did not, so every passage, table, puzzle and picture question read as unanswerable (11 of that day's 29 findings). The workflow uploads `audits/` as an artifact and still opens the PR when the Claude Code step fails (it did on Nov 9, in under a second, and the report was lost with the runner). Run it by hand with `export_open` to write the findings still open on /admin to the log without auditing. To close findings from a session, commit `audits/resolutions/<date>.json` (`[{ id: "<exam>__<questionId>", status: "fixed" | "dismissed", note }]`): when it reaches main the workflow's `resolve` job runs `question-audit.ts --resolve` on it, the same write as the Fixed and Not a problem buttons (a fixed one also closes that question's open reports and restarts its statistics). Since Nov 10 2026 the fix PR carries that file itself, so merging it closes its findings. Check a finding against the official source before applying it: three of that run's were wrong (Manitoba multi-lane changes, the ADI part 2 27 m eyesight distance, R.A. 8551 s. 28).
- **Search gaps** (`scripts/search-gaps.ts`, daily from `.github/workflows/search-gaps.yml`, 07:41 UTC, same secrets): lists the searches that still find no test, using the Searches tab's own grouping (`lib/server/searchGroups.ts`, shared with `/api/admin/searches`) minus anything Planned or Ignored. Claude Code then adds `SEARCH_ALIASES` for searches that name a test we have (each with a case in `tests/test-search-aliases.test.cjs`), builds at most one new test with the `exam-bank` skill for the most-searched missing exam (2+ searches in 30 days by default; only after the skill's step 0 research finds the owner's official outline, handbook or primary law, recorded in `sources.md` and passed by `tools/check_sources.py`, then a 25-question re-check of keys against those sources), marks that search Planned so the next run does not start it again, and opens a PR. A PR that changed only `lib/testCatalog.ts` and `tests/test-search-aliases.test.cjs` (aliases, no new test) merges itself once `scripts/automerge-checks.sh` passes; a PR with a new test or any other file always waits for John. Run it by hand with `build` to force a named test.
- **The Questions tab** on `/admin` (`components/admin/QuestionsTab.tsx`, `/api/admin/questions` uncached, built by `lib/server/questionHealth.ts`, question text from `classQuestionLookup()`): tiles, open audit findings (Fixed / Not a problem), open reports with the reporter's pick (Fixed / Dismiss), statistical flags with the top wrong option marked and the audit's verdict under each (cleared ones folded away; reports show the verdict too), changed and broken source pages (Reviewed), dated changes due, soon or later, and exams due a yearly review. `PATCH` closes reports and findings, clears a flag (`kind: "flag"`) and accepts source pages. The daily brief gets the same counts as `questionQuality` and is told a likely wrong key or a due exam change is worth a bullet.
- `/privacy` covers the reports, the per-question pick totals and Anthropic checking questions.

### Running without John (Nov 13 2026)

The aim is that John hears from the site only when a decision is his. Rules in `lib/ownerWatch.ts` (pure, pinned by `tests/owner-watch.test.cjs`), reads in `lib/server/ownerWatch.ts`.
- **Self-merging PRs**: source triage, the question audit (after a second independent review) and alias-only search-gap PRs merge themselves when their checks pass (see Question quality). New tests, and anything a check or the second reviewer objects to, wait for John. `scripts/automerge-checks.sh` is the one list of those checks: add a check there when adding one to `checks.yml` or `question-banks.yml`.
- **Watchdog** (`/api/cron/watchdog`, daily 11:30 UTC): every scheduled job leaves a timestamp (`readJobHeartbeats()`: `globalStats/studyTimes.updatedAt` for aggregate-stats, `globalStats/questionFlags.updatedAt`, the newest `sourceWatch.checkedAt`, the newest `questionAudits.at` within 9 days, the newest `dailyBriefs.generatedAt` within 4 days). A job past its limit emails John once (transactional, `CAMPAIGN_REPLY_TO`), and again only if still broken 3 days later or another job stops (`shouldAlert()`); state and current problems in `analytics/watchdog`. List a job only when every finished run writes its timestamp, and add a heartbeat with each new nightly job. `?dry=1` reports without emailing.
- **Owner digest** (`/api/cron/owner-digest`, Mondays 12:10 UTC, `buildOwnerDigest()`): one email with only what needs a person: stopped jobs, open pull requests (GitHub's public API through `VERCEL_GIT_REPO_OWNER` / `VERCEL_GIT_REPO_SLUG`; optional `GITHUB_TOKEN` only lifts the rate limit), source pages the triage left as needs_person, audit findings and student reports still open after 2 days (`SETTLE_DAYS`, the time the daily run has to settle them), uncleared flags, exam changes due within 30 days and yearly reviews due. A quiet week still sends "nothing needs you this week", so no email on a Monday means the digest broke. `?dry=1` returns it without sending. Neither route is a campaign, so `CAMPAIGN_CRON_COUNT` is unchanged.
- **Production errors** are read by a Claude scheduled task (daily, Vercel connector), not by code in this repo: it reads the last day's runtime errors and failed cron invocations, opens a PR for a clear bug and stays silent otherwise.

### SEO conventions

- **More than one indexable page per exam (Nov 10 2026).** Besides its landing, an exam can have: (1) **state landings** for the 42 state versions of real estate, life and health and P&C at `<family landing>/<state-name>` (`/real-estate-practice-test/california`; `lib/stateLandings.ts`, `components/exam/StateExamLanding.tsx`, three `[state]` routes), built from the variant's `ExamConfig`, its bank and `lib/stateExamFacts.json`, which `python3 -I tools/build_state_exam_facts.py` generates from the "Official exam facts" table of each `docs/*-state-sources/*.md` (CI runs `--check`; rows over 200 characters stay in the notes); a new variant gets its page automatically. (2) **Topic pages** `/questions/<exam slug>/<topic>` for the v1 exams in `TOPIC_EXAM_IDS` (`lib/topicPages.ts`, one route `app/questions/[exam]/[topic]`): ten bank questions of one category with answers, a topic needing 5 questions (`perTest x testCount`). (3) **Most-missed pages** `/questions/<exam>/most-missed`: the top 5 Common Mistakes with miss rates from `globalStats/examWrongQuestions` (`lib/server/mostMissed.ts`), rendered on first request and revalidated daily, 404 until the exam has the data; the rest of the list stays the Premium tab. (4) **Teacher pages** `/teach/<exam slug>` for every English exam in the `TEACHES` map of `ExamClassFaq` (`lib/teachPages.ts`), whose button opens `/teach/new?exam=<id>`; the class FAQ on each landing links to it. All four are in the `exams` sitemap segment and llms.txt, and landings link to the first two through `ExamRelatedTests` (`ExamTopicLinks`, plus `ExamLanguageLinks` to the same test's other-language landings).
- **Sample questions on every landing.** v1 landings render `<ExamSampleQuestions examId>` (import the bank first), v2 landings `<SampleQuestionsV2 examId>` (`components/v2/SampleQuestionsV2.tsx`: one standalone single-answer question per domain, never from a held-out test), DMV state pages `DmvSampleQuestions` with that state's own questions. Add the two lines to a new landing.
- **Offers and exam costs.** A landing's `WebApplication.offers` is `examOffers(examId, currency?)` (`lib/examSeo.ts`): free practice, plus the Premium price in that currency when the exam has Premium steps. Never write `price: "0"` by hand. `lib/examCosts.json` holds the real exam's fee and retake rule for about 30 exams, each read on the official body's own page (URL kept beside it, `checked` date); `ExamFacts` shows them with an "official source" link and llms-full.txt carries them. Only add a figure read on the official site, and re-check the file when a fee changes (NCLEX rises Feb 1 2027; FINRA and NASAA retake waits change in Jan 2027).
- **Revenue attribution.** Every checkout sends the browser's first-visit `source` and `landing` (`checkoutAttribution()` in `lib/funnel.ts`, falling back to `signupOrigins/{uid}` on the server); the webhook stores both on the `payments` doc, the Today tab shows revenue by source and by landing page, and the GA4 `purchase` event carries `first_source` and `first_landing`. Checkout no longer forces `payment_method_types: ['card']`, so Stripe offers the methods enabled in the dashboard for the buyer's country.
- **Homepage catalog is server-rendered.** `components/TestCatalogStatic.tsx` and `components/TestCard.tsx` (no "use client") render the homepage's cards as plain HTML; only the country pills hydrate. `/tests` keeps the client `TestCatalogGrid`; keep the two groupings in step.

- **One host: www.** Vercel sends `tigertest.io` to `www.tigertest.io`, so every canonical, sitemap, hreflang, JSON-LD, llms.txt and IndexNow URL is built on `SITE_URL` from `lib/siteUrl.ts` (it lifts an apex `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` to www). Never write `process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL || "https://tigertest.io"` in a page again; import `SITE_URL`.
- **Page language.** The one root layout serves `<html lang="en">`; pages under `/es`, `/pt`, `/vi`, `/ko`, `/zh`, `/de`, `/nl` and `/fr` (with the Nordic, Hungarian, Romanian and Greek prefixes) get a `Content-Language` header and `<html lang>` set before first paint from `lib/urlLanguage.ts` (next.config.ts headers and `URL_LANG_INIT_SCRIPT`), and `LanguageContext` keeps a URL's language on its pages.
- **Dates and counts per exam.** A landing's `dateModified`, visible "Updated" line and sitemap `lastmod` come from `lib/landingDates.json` (`npx tsx scripts/build-landing-dates.ts`, git dates of each exam's landing, bank and landing JSON; rerun after committing a landing or bank change, `--check` reports staleness). The catalog's question count is the bank size from `lib/examBankSizes.json`, written by `scripts/build-training-parts.ts`. A DMV state's pass mark is its exact `passCount` in `data/states.ts` (the percent is derived).
- **/about** (`app/about/page.tsx`) says who makes Tigertest, how questions are written, the price and independence; it is linked from the footer and llms.txt and is the Organization's `AboutPage`. The Organization node (`lib/siteSchema.ts`) builds its description from the catalog and `REGIONS`.
- **Country homepages** outside the US carry their own h1 (visually hidden: the top of every homepage shows the same tagline and weekly count as the US page) and lead from `regionHeadline()` (`lib/regionHeadline.ts`, built from the region's hub groups) and region-filtered trending searches.
- **Landing pages** (`app/<x>-practice-test/page.tsx`) are the only exam URLs meant to rank. Each sets its own metadata (title, description, canonical, Open Graph image at `/og/<examId>` from `app/og/[examId]/route.tsx`) and a `WebApplication` + `FAQPage` JSON-LD graph (no breadcrumb: removed Oct 2026, the crumb pushed the headline down and Google no longer shows trails on mobile), and renders `<ExamRelatedTests examId>` before the final CTA (cross-links within the exam's hub group from `relatedTests()` in `lib/testCatalog.ts`). Write metadata and JSON-LD strings with plain apostrophes; `&apos;` only belongs in JSX text.
- **App routes** (`app/<slug>/layout.tsx`) export `examAppMetadata(id)` from `lib/examSeo.ts`: the exam's own title and `noindex, follow`. They are deliberately not disallowed in `app/robots.ts` so crawlers can read the tag. DMV app routes keep their robots.txt disallow.
- **GEO (AI answer engines)**: every exam landing renders `<ExamFacts examId>` under the hero: a plain answer about the real exam, a facts table and a dated "based on" line with the official source, plus a `WebPage` node with `dateModified` (`EXAM_LANDINGS_UPDATED_AT`). The facts live in `lib/examFacts.ts`; keep them in step with the landing's FAQ and add an entry with each new exam. `/llms.txt` and `/llms-full.txt` are generated from the catalog, exam facts and state data (`lib/llmsTxt.ts`, DMV first, with the 51-state written-test table). The site-wide `Organization` and `WebSite` nodes are in `lib/siteSchema.ts`, rendered once by `app/layout.tsx`; point new JSON-LD at them by `@id`. `app/robots.ts` gives every crawler, AI or not, the same allow/disallow lists. First visits are counted by source (`visitSource()` in `lib/funnel.ts`: ChatGPT, Perplexity, Gemini, Copilot, Claude, search, direct, other), shown on the Growth tab. DMV state pages are deliberately left as they are.
- **Sample questions** (`components/exam/ExamSampleQuestions.tsx`): the 29 non-US driving test landings show six bank questions (the last of each category in ID order, sign-image and "A and B" items skipped, options rotated so keys spread over A to D) with answers and explanations in plain HTML plus a `Quiz` JSON-LD node, for "<test> questions and answers" searches and AI answers. The page imports its own bank (`import "@/lib/banks/<id>"`), so nothing else bundles it; add both lines to a new country's landing. `ExamRelatedTests` links a country's exam up to its country homepage, and country homepages list each local test's `EXAM_FACTS` answer under "About each test". `/llms.txt` has a generated "Practice tests by country" section (`countrySection()` in `lib/llmsTxt.ts`).
- **IndexNow** tells Bing, which ChatGPT search reads, about changed pages. After every production deploy, `.github/workflows/indexnow.yml` (on Vercel's `deployment_status`, also runnable by hand with a `days` input) runs `scripts/indexnow-recent.mjs`, which reads the live sitemap and sends every URL whose `lastmod` is within 2 days, using the public key; no secrets. So a launch reaches Bing within minutes, as long as its `lib/landingDates.json` or `lib/seoDates.ts` date was bumped. The nightly cron calls `/api/indexnow/ping?days=2` (`lib/indexNow.ts`); without `days` the route sends every sitemap URL (Nov 2026: before this, launches waited up to a day for the nightly full resubmit). The key file is served at the host root, `/<key>.txt` (a rewrite in `next.config.ts` to `/api/indexnow/key`), because a key file's folder limits which URLs it may submit.
- **Footer** internal links appear on landing pages only and stay inside the test's cluster (`relatedTests` on an exam landing, popular states on DMV landing and state pages); every footer links the `/tests` hub, never all the exams.
- **Sitemap** is an index (`app/sitemap.xml/route.ts`) over per-family files in `app/sitemaps/[segment]/route.ts` (`dmv`, `dmv-es-vi-ko` (which also holds the Chinese pages; the name is kept so Search Console keeps its history), `exams`; `/teach` is in `exams`) built by `lib/sitemaps.ts`, so Search Console shows DMV coverage separately. `lastmod` comes from the hand-bumped dates in `lib/seoDates.ts`; bump the relevant constant when a page family's content changes.

These pages are the DMV flow, not the CDL one: `lib/examRoutes.ts` (`DMV_ROUTES` / `getExamRoutes(id)`) parameterises the shared pieces (`hooks/useTestResults.ts`, `components/results/*`, `components/MissDrill.tsx`, `components/dashboard/ProgressCard.tsx`, `lib/missedQuestions.ts`, `components/AttemptChart.tsx`) by route base, test IDs, pass line, and premium gating. Defaults reproduce DMV behaviour exactly; when changing the DMV results/drill/dashboard flow, keep every exam working.

Theme colors come from the `[data-theme="<examId>"]` blocks in `app/globals.css`; `contexts/TestThemeContext.tsx` and `components/SiteHeader.tsx` pick the theme by path.

**Navigation (Nov 2026).** One header, one in-test bar and one footer everywhere. `components/SiteHeader.tsx` (in the root layout) is full width: the logo (`components/BrandLockup.tsx`, to `/`), TESTS / TEACH tabs (`/` and `/teach`; hidden below `sm` except on `/home-v2`), a **test chip** naming the test on screen (DMV with the state, a v1 or v2 exam), else the current test, none on `/home-v2`, `/tests` and the country homepages, which opens `components/TestSearchSheet.tsx` (the homepage search via `searchAllTests`, the person's started tests first, searches logged through `lib/searchTracking.ts` like the homepage's); then the landing language switcher where one applies, Your Stats, the admin shield and an account menu (Settings, Your classes when `useTeaches()`, Sign out) or a Sign in / Sign up to save pill (left to the dashboard's save card there). It replaced the DMV, exam and v2 headers, the All tests menu and the home-v2 top bar. `isRunnerPath()` hides it wherever someone is answering questions (DMV, v1 and v2 tests and practice, quiz play); results pages keep it. Those runners draw `components/FocusBar.tsx` (✕ to the dashboard, title, where they are, one right slot for the stats upsell, a clock or shuffle, a thin progress bar; practice tests ask before leaving), the same shape as the v2 runners' own top bar. `components/Footer.tsx` is one short footer (made by, country, language, theme, Make a quiz, For teachers, All tests, Privacy), with one line of internal links on landing pages only: popular states on the DMV landing and state pages, the exam's `relatedTests` on an exam landing.

Every header logo is `components/BrandLockup.tsx`, the same everywhere: the DMV tiger (`/tiger.png`, or the crowned `/tiger_face_01.png` for a Premium account via `useIsPremium()`) and "Tigertest", never a test's own tiger or name. It goes to `/`; the test chip is the way back to a test. The current test is the last dashboard opened (DMV with or without a state, any registry exam, any v2 exam), stored as `primaryExam` + `primaryExamAt` in `useStore`, written to `users/{uid}` on its own by `setPrimaryExam` (the newer visit wins on sign-in, cleared on logout) and read through `useCurrentTest()` in `hooks/useHomePath.ts`. Landing pages, tests and results never change it; the header chip shows it on pages of no test, neutral pages (`/tests`, `/settings`, `/admin`) wear its header, and the emails talk about it. `tools/build_logo_svgs.py` still writes the older per-test lockups as standalone SVGs to `public/logos/<id>.svg` and `<id>-dark.svg` (DMV states under `public/logos/dmv/`) for off-site use.

### Stageshow demos

Animated product illustrations are built with the Stageshow skill (`.claude/skills/stageshow`, MIT, installed with `npx skills add benjitaylor/skills --skill stageshow`; `skills-lock.json` pins it). Its React kit is copied to `components/stageshow/kit/` (brand-mark artwork removed); demos live in `components/stageshow/demos/`, each a `"use client"` component with its own CSS scoped under one class: `CreateQuizDemo` (the `/create` hero: a chat makes a quiz, the link card opens into the quiz on a phone) and `TeachClassDemo` (the `/teach` hero: a student's wrong answer on a phone rolls the counts on the teacher's class page; below 640px a phone-led design with a small class-page card). Both play once in view and show their `poster` under reduced motion; the stage grounds have `.dark` overrides while the screens stay light. Read the skill's SKILL.md and `references/` before building or changing one, and film it with `scripts/film.mjs` (needs `playwright-core`; point `CHROME_PATH` at `/opt/pw-browsers/...` in cloud sessions). Its "kit override" lines on these pages are a false positive: Next bundles the kit's layered CSS, which the checker can't tell from a demo's own.

### Languages

Two separate languages. The **interface language** is the person's own (`useStore.language`, synced to `users/{uid}.language` with `languageChosen`, mirrored to the `tt-lang` cookie in `lib/language.ts`): every page follows it except a test's own pages, picked in the footer or on `/settings`; until someone picks, their country's language applies (`REGIONS[].lang`). Only landings whose URL is in a language keep that language (`lib/pageLanguage.ts`). The **test language** is what a test's questions are in: only the languages that exam really exists in (`examLanguages()`, `dmvLanguagesFor(state)` for the DMV), chosen with `TestLanguageChoice` on its dashboard and kept per exam in `useStore.testLanguages` (key `dmv` or the exam id); with no pick it follows the interface language when the exam has it, else the exam's own (`hooks/useTestLanguage.ts`). A test's own pages (DMV app routes, a registry exam's dashboard, tests, training, results, stats) run entirely in its test language (`testPageLanguage()`), so the footer picker is hidden there. Never machine-translate a question bank just to offer a language. Machine-translated and model-written copy and banks are final (John, Nov 12 2026): no human or native review is planned, so the "pending native review" notes in this file and in code comments are historical, and nothing should be held back or flagged to the user for review. Homepages are written in all seventeen languages (`components/home/homeCopy.ts` for en and de, `components/home/copy/<lang>.ts` for the rest, machine-translated pending native review, each with its country names and, for es, pt and fr, `inNames` with the article a sentence needs). A homepage renders in its country's language; once someone picks another one, `middleware.ts` (matcher: every country homepage) rewrites the request to `app/home-lang/[region]/[lang]` (noindex, canonical the country page; crawlers carry no cookie) and the page swaps `REGION_HOME`'s lines for the copy's generic `countryLead` and `testsFor`. `LanguageContext.setLanguage` calls `router.refresh()` so a pick redraws the page. `lib/homeLanguages.ts` lists the homepage languages and the cookie name and must stay import-light (middleware). Test cards link to a test's landing in the page's language when it has one.

### Dark mode

Class strategy (`darkMode: ["class"]`): `contexts/ThemeContext.tsx` keeps the preference (`light` / `dark` / `system`) in localStorage under `tigertest-theme` (a device setting, never synced to the account), toggles `dark` on `<html>` and follows the OS while on `system`. `THEME_INIT_SCRIPT` runs inline in `app/layout.tsx` `<head>` so a dark reader never sees a white flash; `<html>` carries `suppressHydrationWarning` for that reason. `components/ThemeToggle.tsx` is the picker in the footer (never the header), and `ThemeSegmentedControl` the one on `/settings`; labels are under `theme.*` in `i18n/*`.

Components keep their light classes and carry a `dark:` companion, one palette throughout: page `bg-gray-50` becomes `dark:bg-gray-950`, cards `bg-white` become `dark:bg-gray-900`, insets `bg-gray-100` become `dark:bg-gray-800`, borders `gray-200` become `dark:gray-800`, body text `gray-900` becomes `dark:gray-50`, secondary `gray-600` becomes `dark:gray-300`; tinted boxes go to the deep end of their hue (`bg-green-50 dark:bg-green-950`, `text-green-700 dark:text-green-300`); black CTA buttons (`bg-black text-white`) invert to white; translucent whites and blacks, solid brand and semantic fills, and `text-gray-400` stay as they are; hero gradients end on `dark:to-transparent`. The shadcn tokens in the `.dark` block of `globals.css` use the same grays. `tools/add_dark_variants.py` applies those rules mechanically and is idempotent, so run it after adding a page or component that uses hardcoded palette classes (or write the `dark:` companion by hand). Per-theme brand tints for dark (`.dark [data-theme=...]`, brand-light, gradient-to, border-light darken; brand-dark and brand-darker lighten; brands under 50% lightness lift to 50%) are generated by `tools/gen_dark_brand_css.py`; re-run it when adding a theme block. v2 exams get the same tints at runtime from `lib/v2/darkTheme.ts` through `V2Shell`, and `components/v2/v2.css` carries the `.dark .v2-shell` / `.dark .v2-focus` overrides. v2 figures are grayscale SVGs and are shown with `dark:invert`.

### Privacy and legal (Nov 2026)

`lib/privacyRegion.ts` is the one place for location- and age-based privacy rules. Google Analytics runs only when allowed: `ANALYTICS_INIT_SCRIPT` (inline in `app/layout.tsx` `<head>`, before gtag) sets GA's kill switch `window["ga-disable-<id>"]`, and `components/AnalyticsConsent.tsx` (root layout) keeps it current, learns the country once from `/api/geo` (cookie `tt-cc`) and shows the cookie banner in the EEA, UK and Switzerland until the visitor chooses (`tt-consent`; `/privacy` has a button that reopens it for anyone). An unknown country (`ZZ`) gets the same opt-in. The first `gtag('config')` in `app/layout.tsx` is skipped while the kill switch is on, because GA drops the page view of a config sent then and a later config does not bring it back; `AnalyticsConsent` sends the config once GA is allowed, so a first-time visitor's page view and its source are kept. GA is always off on children's tests (`CHILD_PATH_PREFIXES`: times tables, KS2, 11+, OC, NSW Selective, NAPLAN Year 7, JNVST, AISSEE, `/c/`) and for any browser with a class or quiz membership; add a new primary or middle-school exam's slug and landing there. A declined choice also stops the anonymous counters (`isTestBrowser()` checks `analyticsDeclined()`). The signup email box and the Google sign-in paths use `defaultEmailConsent()`: unticked in the EEA, UK, CH and Canada or when the country is unknown. Settings has Delete account (`components/DeleteAccountSection.tsx`, `/api/account/delete`: users doc, quizzes, classes taught, memberships, assistant play, enrollments, signup origin, OAuth tokens, then the Auth user; payments are kept for tax). Marketing email footers carry `postalFooterLine()` (`lib/emailPostal.ts`, env `EMAIL_POSTAL_ADDRESS`, required by CAN-SPAM). `/privacy` and `/terms` describe what the code does; when a feature collects new data or adds a processor, update `/privacy` and its date.

### Email

**Links in any email John sends or Claude drafts** (templates, Gmail drafts, replies to users): always write the full URL, `https://www.tigertest.io/<path>`. Never a bare `tigertest.io/...`, never the apex host, never a path without the scheme. Build template links on `SITE_URL` from `lib/siteUrl.ts`. Check every link in a draft before saving it. **Never put a link in a Gmail draft made through the Gmail connector**: it rewrites every link it saves (plain text and `<a href>` alike, reply or new draft) to `https://www.google.com/url?q=...&source=gmail&ust=...`, which goes out broken. For any email with a link, give John the paste-ready text in chat instead. If a draft is ever made anyway, read it back with `get_draft` in `RAW` format and check every `href` before calling it done.

Resend, from `noreply@tigertest.io`, reply-to John. Templates are HTML strings in `lib/email-templates.ts`, one function of an `EmailVoice` (`lib/email-voice.ts`) each. The signup welcome (`/api/send-welcome-email`) is about the exam in the signup URL (`examIdFromSignupUrl`: the `?redirect=` target, else the page, else the store's `primaryExam`), and seeds `primaryExam` only when the account has none; a signup from `/teach` (`isTeacherSignupUrl`) gets `welcomeTeacher` instead and no exam is set. The sender is `lib/server/welcomeEmail.ts`, shared with the hourly `/api/cron/welcome-backstop`, which sends the welcome to every account made 15 minutes to 24 hours ago whose browser call never arrived (no `welcome` in `emailsSent`; opted-out skipped; copy from its `primaryExam`). Every signup path calls the route with `keepalive`, including Google and email signups on the connector's `/oauth/login` page. It is transactional mail, not a campaign, so it does not count in `CAMPAIGN_CRON_COUNT`. Daily campaign crons under `app/api/cron/*` (schedules in `vercel.json`) share `lib/cron-email.ts`: one Auth list call, `getEligibleUsers()` (consent, opt-out, quiet-days rule per exam), a 24h frequency cap, a once-only `emailsSent` key per campaign, and the account-wide daily budget in `lib/email-quota.ts` (bump `CAMPAIGN_CRON_COUNT` when adding a cron). `scripts/email-dryrun.mjs` shows who each cron would reach without sending.

**The question that got away** (`/api/cron/exit-question`, 18:00 UTC, key `exitQuestion`, once ever): someone whose last act was a training answer (`findAccountExit()` in `lib/exitQuestions.ts`, the Exit questions card's rules: 3+ answers, no test passed on that exam, last act not a finished test) and who has been quiet 3 to 10 days gets that question back with every option, the answer in green, the explanation and a joke (`exitQuestionEmail()`; one copy for a miss, one for a right answer), about the exam the question came from. English readers only, DMV and v1 exams only (v2 keeps no answer times); stamps `exitQuestionEmail` {examId, questionId, isCorrect}. Paused unless `EXIT_QUESTION_CAMPAIGN=send`; paused or `?dry=1` it sends nothing and returns the eligible count and sample question ids.

**Top prepper** (`/api/cron/top-prepper`, 16:20 UTC, key `topPrepper`, once ever; rules in `lib/topPrepper.ts`, pinned by `tests/top-prepper.test.cjs`): an unpaid English reader who has answered 100+ questions and more than 75% of all accounts with any answer (`_stats`, read for every account so the percentile is honest), and whose latest paywall view was 36 hours to 14 days ago, gets their own numbers ("more than 92% of the people who practice here"), the paywall they opened by name, Premium's price where they are (the country of their last paywall view, `paywallCountry`, else of their signup; no price when neither is known) and a button to `<dashboard>?unlock=1`, which opens the paywall on the DMV dashboard and `ExamDashboard` once the account has loaded. About the exam of that paywall, DMV and v1 only (`_stats` has no v2 answers); class students of that exam are skipped. One account in ten (stable by uid) is held out and stamped `topPrepper.group = "holdout"` instead of mailed, only as far down the ranked list as that day's sends reached; a send stamps `group: "sent"` with `at`, `examId`, `answers`, `pct`. The Conversion tab's "Does the top prepper email work?" card compares purchases within 7 days for the two groups. Paused unless `TOP_PREPPER_CAMPAIGN=send`; paused or `?dry=1` it sends and stamps nothing and returns the eligible count and a sample. Never state a percentile that is not computed from live data.

Opt-out is `users/{uid}.unsubscribed` (`lib/unsubscribe.ts`). It is checked in the audience query and again in `sendCronEmail()` right before every send, and every campaign carries RFC 8058 `List-Unsubscribe` headers pointing at `/api/unsubscribe` (GET and one-click POST). Nothing on the client writes that field. The new-tests campaign (`newTests2026`) is paused unless `NEW_TESTS_CAMPAIGN=send`; while paused each daily run only logs how many people it would reach. DMV readers are split across the variants still sending in `lib/email-launch.ts` (v4 and v5; v1 and v3 retired Oct 17 via `RETIRED_VARIANTS`) (one per user, stable by uid, stamped as `emailVariants.newTests2026` on send; links go through `/api/email/click`, which records `emailClicks.newTests2026`). The split-test card on the Growth tab was removed Oct 30; the stamps are still written. Non-DMV readers get the short version, outside the test. It also asks "what should we build next?": clicks land in the `testRequests` collection via `/api/feedback/test-request` and `/request-test` (`lib/test-requests.ts`), shown on the Growth tab; a named exam emails John immediately.

### Mobile Apps

`/mobile/` contains a Capacitor 8 shell (app id `io.tigertest.app`) that loads
the production site in a native WebView for iOS and Android. The site detects
the shell via a `TigerTestApp` user-agent token (`lib/native-app.ts`,
`hooks/useIsNativeApp.ts`) and hides Google Sign-In and Stripe checkout
(store-policy compliance) and applies safe-area CSS. After changing
`mobile/capacitor.config.ts`, run `npx cap sync` in `/mobile` and commit the
result. Store submission guide: `docs/APP_STORE_DEPLOYMENT.md`.

### Agentic Question Rewrite System

`/agentic-rewrite/` contains a multi-agent pipeline for generating and validating questions. A supervisor agent orchestrates generators and four validator agents (distribution, memorization, format, answer-length) with a fixer agent for corrections. State question files live in `/agentic-rewrite/states/`.

## Environment Variables

```
RESEND_API_KEY                  # Email sending (Resend)
FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY    # Server-side Firebase admin SDK (JSON)
NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL            # Site URL for metadata
ANTHROPIC_API_KEY               # Claude API, for the daily update on the /admin Today tab (cron skips when unset)
CRON_SECRET                     # Bearer token for /api/cron/* and /api/indexnow/ping
INDEXNOW_KEY                    # 8–128 char hex key for IndexNow (Bing) submissions
EMAIL_REPLY_TO                  # Reply-To on campaign mail and test-request notifications (default john@tigertest.io)
CRON_MAX_BATCH                  # Per-run campaign cap (default: daily campaign budget / cron count)
NEW_TESTS_CAMPAIGN              # "send" turns on the SAT/ACT launch email; anything else (or unset) = paused, logs a daily dry-run count
EXIT_QUESTION_CAMPAIGN          # "send" turns on the question-that-got-away email (/api/cron/exit-question); anything else (or unset) = paused, returns the eligible count
TOP_PREPPER_CAMPAIGN            # "send" turns on the top prepper email (/api/cron/top-prepper); anything else (or unset) = paused, returns the eligible count
NEW_TESTS_WINNER                # Optional launch-email variant id (v4 or v5, a retired id is ignored): sends everyone not yet emailed that variant
QUIZZES_CAMPAIGN                # "send" turns on the quizzes launch email (/api/cron/quizzes-launch); anything else (or unset) = paused, logs a daily eligible count
MCP_TOKEN_SECRET                # HS256 signing key for the MCP server's access tokens (rotating it logs every client out)
EMAIL_POSTAL_ADDRESS            # Postal address in marketing email footers (CAN-SPAM), e.g. a PO box
MCP_OAUTH_ISSUER_URL            # Public URL of the MCP server / site (https://tigertest.io): JWT issuer and the base of the links tools return
GITHUB_TOKEN                    # Optional: lifts GitHub's rate limit when the Monday owner digest lists open pull requests
```

**MCP study continuation**: `list_in_progress_tests` discovers only the caller’s synced account progress (site started-test signals, plus learned Basics/custom quiz history). `get_study_progress`, `start_study_practice`, and `submit_study_answer` add v1 registry and v2 drill practice without replacing legacy DMV or custom-quiz tools. Catalog practice updates `users/{uid}.trainingSets`/history; v2 uses its existing drills and grader. Pending attempt IDs prevent stale answer replay. Timed/adaptive sittings remain web-only and unchanged. Browser sync is not live collaboration. MCP writes increment per-store mcpProgressRevisions (catalog/v2/quiz); browser save transactions reject stale revisions and latch a visible conflict until explicit reload. This preserves newer cloud progress without guessing reset/delete merge intent; unsynced browser work may need repeating. Scope baselines are captured before local replacement, never refreshed mid-session. No model calls.
